FORESTRY SCHEME.
Mr Forbes, Chief Forestry Inspector of till,e Department of
Agriculture, -was then introduced to the Committee by the
Chairman, and briefly explained the linos •on which the Fores-
try Séhemes 'were worked, and whi071 he stated were formula-
ted in 1906. 'He explained that woodlands of 50 aeros and
over wore the only ones entitled to Departmental •Grants; t\hat
the average of caretaking, incl uding re-planting, was
a bout El per acre, and that the sum of 5s per acre would be
about the maximum cccst for caretaking without re-pla.nting ;
that the consent of the Department would be necessary before
could 'be effect•cd, and that grants would be available
from thre Development Fund for maintenance when certified by
the I) epartment. He stated that it would be necessary for the
County Council to strike a small rate of about one halfpenny
in the e for one year, and that the amount realised from this
lodged in a separate Fund with the County Tre:wurer,
bearing interest at 2 per cent. He also stated that the wood-
land known as Omeath Park, which he Inad •inspected himself,
would be a most desirable one to acquire under a Con.nty Louth
Forestry 'Sdheme ; that County Kildare had Obtained grants
under their scheme of C 1,600, and that if County Louth, being
the second county, came forward with their application
now, the grmnt would likely be as generous, O.nd iilso that the
present was an opportune time, as the period for -which grants
are to be made begins this year, and County Louth, by adopt-
ing their Forestry Scheme, would now be 'eligible for participa-
tion.
Mr 'Goodman opposed the Scheme, as the purchase money
would come out of the rates.
Mr '(V Meara stated that. the purchase could be effected
through the Land Judge;' Court, and the transfer made by the
Land Commission, and that the actual costs to the rates would
be comparatively small. This view was substantiated by Mr.
F orbes.
Eventually the Committee tunanimously deeiclcd to proceed
wit'h the purchase of Orneath Park, ancl to include in the
scheme Williston-n plantation, and also whatever portion of
the Ruxton grcuncls at Ardee that the County Council would
arrange t.o purd.ha.se.
The Chairman thanked Mr Forbes for coming down to assist
them, and for t:he information and advice he had given in the
matter.

COLLECTION OF MOTOR CAR LICENCE DUTIES.
The following letter from the Road Board was read :—
The Road Pezard,
Westminster, London,
10th March, 1914.
Sir,
I am directed by the Road Board to refer to the re-
cent, «wrespondence that has taken place respecting the col-
of Motor Car Licence Duties, and to enclose herewith a
memorandum that has 'been prepared by the direction of the
Board, it is hoped will prove of assistance to County
Councils in carrying out their statutory obligations in this mat-
I am Vo ask that you will be good enough €0 read this letter,
ancl explain the contents of the memorandum to the next
meeting of your County Council, ancl thereafter acquaint the
Bowrd with the action taken by the County Council thereon,
and the steps proposed to be taken to secure. the proper col-
lection of the duties. In particular I am to inquire whether
prosecutions have been instituted against the owners of motor
vehicles in respect of arrears of •duties which accrued during
1913, •or for other offences in connection with the collection of
the duties.
It will be olßerved that the memorandum divides itself in
tW0 parts, the first being addressed particularly €0 the County
Councils, while the second part is in the nature of suggested
instructions to the officials, who it is assumed have already
been or will be appointed by the County Councils t.o carry out
the work of the collection of the duties. "Ilme Board hope that
the Council Mill by resolution direct their officers to give effect
to these instructions.
46
I need hardly add that in the opinion of the mard a radical
alteration of the methods employed for tile collection of Motor
Car License Duties is necessary in many counties in view of
tho fact that the amount of duties collected in Ireland in
1910-11, viz, C19,Oö7 (only a fraction of the amount which
should have bren collected) has fallen to even lower in
the years 1911-12 and 1912-13, notwithstanding the in
the number of motor vehicles in 'Ilse.
I am, Sits
Your obedient Servant,
W. REES JEFFREYS,
Seoletan•y.
The Secretary to the Louth County Council,
Courthouse, Dundalk.
The memorandum enc•lcsed with this letter was a lengthy
document giving detailed directions Vo County Councils and
their officers as to the methods to 'be employed for the efficient
collection of the Motor' Car License Duties. It set out that
it was desirable to appoint an officer or officers whose sole or
principal duties it would be to carry out the statutory duties
imposed on County Councils in respect to the collection of the
duties, and to give sud-l officers facilities for travelling so
that, all owners and other motor ear users resident in tho
county, who are suspected of not having paid the duties,
might be visit(d. Various matters relating to registrations,
declarations, and methods of measuring the horse-power of
motors, and systems of inter-comm.unica tion between County
Councils were embodied in the Memorandum.
The Committee authorised the Secretary to formally appoint
Mr. Joseph Louden as his and t).hat he would have
authority to make inspeeticns throughout the county in con-
nection with the collection of duties, service of not.iees, or any
other necessary matters relating to same.
47
The •Seeretar.v was also directed to have circular issued to
all Motorists who hacl not paid the Licence Duty to have this
done ln•ore Bhe end of the present month, and to proceed with
despatch in the collection of the duties.

INSURANCE COMMISSIONERS A-ND MEDICAL
AI)VISERS.
The following Resolution, submitted 'by the King's County
Council, was marked •read" :
That this C%rnmi€tee, acting on behalf of the County
Council of King's County, hereby unanimously and emphatie-
a Ily approve of the resolution passed on this subject by the
King's Coun€.y Insurance Committee as follows
"That we consider the proposals of tthe National ln.smranee
Commission appointing the Medical Advisers as the sole
anthority in connection with the certification of Tuberculosis
patients on Form 2 as most inadvisable, as we eonsider they
would make the administra Lion of Sanatorium benefit most
unworkable, and create a strong feeling of animosity amongst
the medical profession of the county. We consequently decline
to enforce the scheme as submitted by the Commissioners, ancl
we further declare it to be' our emphatic opinion that the
exclusion of the medical practitioncus, or imposition of Medical
Advisers as appointed by the National Insurance Commis-
50
sioners, would break up the whole system of Sanatoritliit
benefits, whether administered by the Insurance Commissioners
itin,dor the Insurance Act of 1911, of 'by bhe County Council
under the Tuberculosis Prevention Act of 1908. Copies of
this Resolution to be sent €0 the Local Government Board
and the National Insurance Commission."

CARLINGFORD PIER. LAMP.
The question of Chrlingford Pier Lamp was again under
discussion, and Mr. Lyons, acting County Surveyor, produced
before the Committee the new la.rnp that had been pu rehased.
Ile explained that he was anxious to demonstrate the fact that
notwithstanding any statement to the contrary the lighting
of the lamp was an easy matter. He then proceeded to light
the lamp.
It was pointed out by Mr. William Woods thxt t.he lighting
of the hvmp in the frame in a chamber like the County Council
was entirely different from the lightin,g of the lamp as it
would be in the standard on the Pier at Carlingford, especially
when the weather was stormy. He also stated that even if
the lamp were taken out of the frame and carried to till hut
to be filled and lighted, it would be impossible to keep it
lighted until it was replaced in the frame.
The Committee generally considered that the lamp was
rather unsuitable for the purpose it was required for, but no
order was made.

BURNING OF SHEI) ON CARLINGFORD PIER.
The following letters from the Harbour Constable, Carling-
ford, were read. The Secretary explained that when he re-
eeived the first letter, he wrote to the Constable for a formal
report as to how the aecident occurred, and the letter clated
the 23rd instant v,as his reply.
Carlingford, 20th March, 1914.
Charles M' Alester. Esq.
Dear Sir.
I request to inform you that tar-boarded oil hut
on old pier, Carlingford, accidentally caught fire on last night,
and was burned :and all its contents, only the new lamp saved,
which happened to be erected on pier head.
JOHN FEARON,
H. Constable.
Carlingford, 23rd March, 1914.
Dear Sir.
Yours of 21st instant reeoivecl this morning. In reply
the fire must have occurred this way: When I lit the new lamp
I muse have dropped the half-lighted match cn the floor,
was saturated witli Oil, and when I earne baek from pier head
after having Pilt up new lamp the hut was afire. I rushed in
trying to get out oil barrel, was nearly caught in the
flames. no help near, the only things destroyed, the oil barrel,
in which there might have been about three or fotr, at most,
gallons of oil, and the four lamps which were in use on old
quay, and of course the tar boards of the hut. I need not say
how easily the hut, Wllioh was tarred eve,ry altet•njatx• year for
the past 40 years, caught fire ; the tvhole thing was over in a
few minutes.
The w.e. recently repaired, which was attached to oil hut, all
right ; no harm done to it.
Yours faithfu Ily,
JOHN FEARON.
Ila:rbour Constable.
Charles M' Alester, Secretary.
Louth County Council.

ancl substantial stone building which would proper stc,r-
age for imports of coal, etc, and wr,ald aet as a feeder to the
port. Eventually tho following su b-eommitt,ee was appointeo
to visit Carlingford on Thursday, the 2nd and report ou

The following letter from two of the Road Rolling Staff, who
aet as flagmen, was reaxl
Proleek, Ravew«lale,
23rd Mmh, 1914.
To Presiding Chairman,
Louth County Council.
Dear Sir,
We, the undersigned flagmen in your employment,
beg to appeal to your consideration for an increase in our
wage, thirteen shillings per week, living out in all kinds of wea-
ther, away from home. This we consider quite inadequate
as a living wage under existing circumstances, price of food,
and also in face of fact that your neighbouring counties,
Arrna.g.h amd Monaghan, pay at the rate of from 16s to 17s per
week wage.
Trusting to you. favourable consideration of this matter, we
beg to subscribe ourselves your obedient servants,
FRANK MOERMOtrr.

SHEEP SCAB ORDER.
The following circular letter from the Department,
No. 242/14. V.B.., was read, enclosing copy ()rder of the
'23rd January, 1914, and the Secretary was directed to
send copi,es of the Order to the Veterinary
Inspectors : —
I)epartncellt of Agriculture and Technical
Instruction for Ireland,
(Veterinary Branch),
51 Upper Mount Street,
March, 1914.
No. 242/14. V. B.
Sir,
I have to forward, to be laid before
your Local Authority, copies of an Order entitled "The
Sheep Scab (Local Regulations) (Ireland) Order. oi
1914," and, at the same time, to invite the earnest
attention of the Local Authorities to the new situation,
which demands that immediate and energetic measures
should be taken by them to advance the object aimed at
by this ()rder, viz., the eradication of Sheep Scab from
their respective districts
The ()rder confers on IOC&I Authorities
additional powers for dealing with this mat-
already
supplementary to the powers
ter,
conferred by previous Orders of the Depalt-
It enables them to make regulations to arrest
ment.
the spread of Sheep Seab ill their districts by regulating
the movement of sheep into them from other districts.
With these powers and those already possessed
bv
73
them; with the aid of the Illsper•tors or officers whom
they employ, or have the power to employ; with the
generaV support of the Royal Irish Constabulary behind
their local officers; and with the further assistance
which the Department are prepared to give them by
means of advice and in the supervision of their staffs,
the Loral Authorities are now in a state of complete
effectiveness for operating against this disease.
tliat is needed to secure success is vigour on their part,
and earnest co-operation from the public of Lhe distriei,
and especially co-operat0i1 from those whose interests
are most directly injured by the spread of the disease
and moot directly benefited by its suppression.
The Department wish to sef before all eoneerned
in each County—both the general public and those en-
trusted with any (lut,y or placed ili any position eon-
li.eeted with the administration of the law in this
in the interests of the
matter the imi porta nee,
country, of au efficient. enforcement of these Orders ili
all their details, including adequate of any
offences against them.
As mentioned above, a new situation has been
ereatecl by the imminent danger that, unless effective
measures are now taken, t,he export trade in Irish sheep
may be subjected to damaging interruption.
Great Britain there is growing a be?ief that the impor-
t,a.tion of Irish sheep. amongst which
eases of the
disease continue to be found, is now the principal, if
not the sole, obstacle to the complete elimi.nat,ion of the
disease i n that eon n,iry. Prejudice has been created
of
against sheep from Ireland, aml the question
regulating or proh ibiting their importation into Great
Britain for the better protection of British flocks has
been raised.
The Department desire. plainly to inform the Local
Authorities that, in their opin ion, unless immediate
steps are taken to clear the country of the disease,
measures of this nature are likely to be declared at an
(late. To save the country generally from the
earl
heavy loss whiteh would thus be involved, and to secure
the valuable improvement to our Irish flocks which
would be the result of clearing out this disease and
restoring to Irish sheep a clean of health, all that
is needed is that the Local Authorities throw them-
selves with vigour in to a common campaign
this pest, and that the farmers and sheep owners eo-
The disease is one, which can be
operate witli them.
completely extirpated simply by the practice of regular
and thorough dipping.
rl'he mall who does not dip his sheep, but allows
them to Ilapse into this unhealthy condition, and to
carry infection with them wherever they go, is not only
guilty of folly in his own personal interests, but is,
without thinking it, a public enemy propagating infee-
tion and inflicting injury aud loss upon his fellow-
farmers and sheep raisers a,nxl all who depend upon the
sheep ra.ising industry in the country.
The purpose of the (Orders is to see that the sheep
of a county are dipped at least twice a year, •and to pro-
vide, where necessary, facilities for (lipping a measure
of the greatest personal advantage to owners of sheep;
to see that where the disease appears it is promptly
notified, so that the proper steps may be taken for its
75
suppression ; and to protect the markets and fairs from
the entry into them of sheep affected with the disease.
Tlme I)epartmen€ desire to draw your Local Authority's
attention to the following ways, in particular, in which
they eau further the object ill view, viz :
(I) By making adequate provision for having the
In the Depart-
Dipping Order fuli'y eoiuplied with.
ment's view it will be necessary, in most instances, to
Oave the Local Authority's Veterina1Y Officers specially
assigned to this duty during the dipping period, and to
appoint, where necessary during that period, persons
to assist them.
(2) By requiring their Inspectors to take the most
prompt action in the treatment of reported eases; and
to exercise the greatest care ill removing restrictions.
(3) By using Che persons employed to assist their
( )ffieers in supervising the dipping, who should be
experienced in the care of sheep, to range their
(lisfriet with a view to the, detection of unreported eases.
(4) By arranging for the systematic inspection by
[heir Veterinary Inspectors of sheep exposed for sale
at markets aud fairs.
(5) Bv the adoption of regulations, under the
enclosed ()rder, where it seems desirable, too regulate the
movement of sheep from an affeefecl into a free district.
(G) By makiumg it apparent that they regard the
concealment of Sheep Scab, the movement of diseased
sheep, and Che failure to clip sheep, as serious offences
eonunitted by private individuals against the general
interest, and that they consider that such offences cali
76
for prosecution and justify the imfåietion of heavy
penalties, and should be condemned by public opinion.
To assist the Local Authorities in the furtherance
of these duties the Department, whose inspectorial staff
has been strengthened for the purpose, have now ar-
ranged to co-operate in supervising the work of the
officers of the Local Authorities employed in the
administration of the Diseases of Animals Acts. 'Illis
provision should materially help the Local Authorities
ill bracing up the general efficiency of their officers
employed under these Aets.
The l)epartmelit will further, should circumstances
permit, be glad to arrange for one of their Superintend-
ing Veterinary Inspectors to confer with your Local
Authority on this subject generally.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient, Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.
The Clerk of
each Loea,lL Authority.
ORDER OF THE DEPARTMENT OF AGRICULTURE A-ND
TECHNICAL INSTRUCTION FOR IRELAND.
(Dated 23rd January, 1914).
THE SHEEP-SCAB (LOCAL REGULATIONS) (IRELAND)
ORDER OF 1914 (No.
The Depai•trnent of Agriculture and Technical Instruction for
Ireland, by virtue ancl in exercise Of the, powers in them vested
under the Agrictplture ancl Technical Instruction (Ireland) Act,
1893, the Diseases of Animals Acts, 1894 to 1911, and of every
other powers enabling them in this behalf, do order, and it is
hereby ordered as follows :—
POWERS OF' A LOCAL AUTHORITY AS TO MAKING
REGULATIONS.
1. A Local Authority may, with a view of preventing Che
spreading of sheep-scab, make such Regulations as they think
fit for the following purposes, or any of them :
(a.) For regulating the movement by land or water of sheep
into their District from the District of any other Local
Authority in Ireland.
(b.) For requiring tille occupier of any premises within their
District to which sheep are moved from outside such Dis-
trict to notify forthwith the arrival •of the sheep to a
Constable or other member of the Police Force at the
nearest Police Baarraek or Station who shall forthwith
transmit thc information to the Clerk of the Local Au-
thority under the Diseases of Animals Acts for such Dis-
trict.
(c.) For prescribing, regulating and securing the dipping
within a specified pelöcl in an approved sheep-dip and in
the presence and to the satisfaction of an Inspector of
the Local Authority of sheep moved into their District
from the District of any other Local Authority in Ire-
land or of sheep imported direct into t;heir District.
78
(d.) For prohibiting or regulating the movement or securing
the isolation of sheep moved into their District from
outside such District unless and until such sheep have
been dipped as prescribed.
PROVISIONS AS TO REGULATIONS OF LOCAL
AUTHORITY.
2. (I.) A I aoca] Authority shall forthwith send to the See-
retar.v, Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction
for Ireland, Dublin, a copy of every Regulation made by tihem
under this Order.
(2.) If the Depoliment are satisfied on inquiry, with respect
to any Regulation or part of a Regulation made by a Local Au-
thority under this that t'he same is for any reason objec-
tional"e, a.ncl divoet the revocation thereof, the Same shall
thereupon cease to operate.
(3.) A Local Authority shall send a copy of every Regula-
tion made by then. in relation to the movement of s leer under
Chis Order to every railway company having a, railway station
within the Distric+ of the Local Authority, or within the part
of their District to which the Regulation applies, and shall also
forthwith send a copy of the Regulation to the Secretary, Rail-
way Cearing House, 5, Kildare Street, Dublin, and to the
Clerk of each, Local Authority in Irela nd.
(4.) For the purpose of any R egulation made by a Local Au-
thority under this Order, an Inspector of such Local Authority
may enter any premises within the District of the Lo-
eal Authority and examine any sheep thereon, and such powers
of entry and examination shall be in addition to any other like
powers vested in the Inspector by the Act of 1894 or other-
wise.
(5.) Any powers extreisable under a Regulation made by a
Local Ant,hority under this Order by a Vetelinary Inspector
acting for a Local Authority shall be exercisable also by a
Veterinary Inspector of the Department.
79
POWER 'ro MAKE REGULATIONS NOT TO BE
DEPUTED.
3. The power to make Regulations under this Order shall be
exercised only by a Local Authority or their Excentive Com-
mitt,ee, ancl shall not be deputed to any other nor
to a Sub-Committee.
LIMITATION AS To REGULATIONS OF l,oc.xm,
AVTUOIUTY.
4. For the of this Order, or of any Regulation made
by a Local Authority thereunder, sheep shall not be cleerned to
be moved from, into, or within the District of a LoealAuthority
or within the part of the District to vvhieh the Regulation np-
plies, where they are moved through the or such part
thereof by railway from a place outside the District, or such
part thereof—
(a.) to another place ou tho District, or such part there-
of, without nn neeessar.y delay and without the sheep
being untrnele«l within the District, or sueå part there-
of; or
(b.) direct to port within the Dist,riet or to a lair at such
port for shipment froin snell port within two days.
OFI•'E.XCES.
5. If a sheep is moved in eontravention of any Regula-
tion made by the Local Anthority under this Order, the owner
Of the sheep, and the person in enarge thereof, and the person
eausi ng, directing, or permitting the movement, and the person
moving or conve>ing the sheep, and the eonsignee or other per-
son receiving or keeping it knowing it to have been moved in
contravention as aforesaid, and the occupier of the place from
which the sheep is moved, s'!all, each according to and in re-•s.
pect of his own acts and defaults, be decinecl guilty of an of-
fence against the Act of 1891.
80
(2.) If the arrival Of a sheep is not notified as required by
any Regula tion made by a Local Authority nncler this Order,
the owner of the sheep, and the person in charge thereof, ancl
the occupier of the place or premises at which the sheep has ar-
rived, shall, each according to and in respect of his own acts
defaults, be deemed guilty of an offence against the Act
of 1894.
(3.) If anytihing is otherwise done or omitted to be done in
contravention of any Regulation made by Local Authority
under this Order, the owner and the lessee and the occupier and
the person in charge of any place, sheep, carcase or thing in or
in respect of which the same is done or omitted to be done,
shall, each according to and in respect of his own acts and de-
faults be deemed guilty of an offence against the Act of 1894.
INERPRETATION.
G. In this Order, unless the context otherwise requires—
' 'Approved -sheep-dip" means a dip approved by the Depart-
ment for sheep scab and sheep dipping in Ireland or pur-
porting to be so approved.
' 'The Department" means the Department, of Agricultura
and Technical InstiTtetion for Ireland.
' 'The Aet of 1894" means the Diseases of Animals Act, 1894.
Other terms have the same meaning as in the Diseases of
Animals Act, 1894, and the Acts amending same.
COMMENCEMENT.
7. This Order Shal] come into operation on the twenty-sixth
SHORT TITLE.
9. This Order may be cited as the Sneep-Seab (Local Regula-
tions) (Ireland) Order of 1914 (No. 2).
In witness whereof the Department of Agriculture and
Technical Instruction for Ireland have hereunto set
their Official Seal this twenty-third day of January,
one Chousand nine hundred and fourteen.
(Signed),
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.

ORDER OF THE DEPARTMENT OF AGRICULTURE A-ND
TECHNICAL INSTR FOR IRELAND.
25th March, 1914).
THE MOVEMENT OF ANIMALS (SOUTHERN IRELAND)
TEMPORARY PROHIBITION ORDER.
The Department of Agriculture and Teehnical Instruction for
Ireland, by virtue and in •exercise of the powers vested in them
under tho Agriculture and Technical Instruction (Ireland) Act,
1899, the Diseases of Animals Acts, 1894 to 1911, and of every
82
other power enabling them in this behalf, do order, and it is
hereby ordered, as follows
PROHIBITION OF MOVEMENT OF ANIMALS.
1. Unless and ,until the Department otherwise order no cat-
tle, sheep, goats, .or swine, shall be moved either by land or by
water out of the area described in the Schedule to this Order.
OPERATION OF OTHER ORDERS.
2. Nothing in this Order shall be deemed to interfere with
the operation of the provisions of Orders of the Department re-
lacing to Foot and Mouth Disease in Ireland.
OFFENCES.
3. If an animal is moved in contravention of this Order, the
owner Of the animal, and the person for the time being in
charge thereof, ancl the person eausing, directing, or permit-
ting the movement, and the person moving or conveying tche
animal, aud the consignee or other person receiving or keeping
it, knowing it to have been moved in contravention as afore-
said, and the occupier of the place from which the animal is
moved, shall ench according to and in re.spcet of his own acts
and defaults, be deemed guilty of an offence against the Disu
eases of Animals Act, 1894.
COMMENCEMENT.
4. This Order shall come into operation. on the twenty-sev
venth day of March, nineteen hundred and fourteen.
SHORT TITLE.
5. This Order may be cited a.s The Movement of Animals
(Southern Ireland) Temporary Prohibition Order.
In witness whereof the Department Of Agricultllre and
Teehnictll Instruction for Ireland have, hereunto set
clay of March,
their Official Seal this twenty-fifth
nineteen hundred and fourteen.
(Signed),
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.

SCHEDULE.
So much of Ireland as lies to the south of the following
boundary (roads and' railway lines mentioned as forming Che
boundary being regarded as outside the Scheduled Area) :
Commencing at the Maiden Tower, near the sea coast east of
Drogheda, thence along the river bank past Mornington to the
Great Northern Railway line at l)'rogheda, thence by that rail-
Way line via Navan to the terminus of the line at Oldcastle,
t'hence by the road leading from Oldcastle Railway Station to
Fore, as far as the junction of Chat road with the direct road
to Granard, thence by the Granard road by Finea Bridge and
north of Bracklagh Lough as far as the Midland Great Western
Railway, north of Ballywillan Station, thence southwards along
the IQilway line to Mullingar, thence westwards by that rail-
way line via Athlone and Ballinasloe to where the railway line
A thenry ,
crosses the road lendi ng from Moni vea through
thence along the road leading €0 Oranmore to the point where
it is crossed by the railway line, thence along the railway line
€0 Oranmore Station, thence in a, southerly direction by the
road leading to Oranmore as far as Oranmore, and thence in a
direct line to the sea at Oranmore Pier.

REPORT OF INSPECTION COMMITTEE APPOINTED BY
FINANCE COMMITTEE FOR CARLINGFORD HARBOUR.
The following report was read :—
The Committee appointed by the Finance Committee to visit
and report on the recent burning accident to the Shed on
Carlingford Pier, and the proposed now Store, met at Carling-
ford on Thursday, the 2nd April, when the following attended :
—Messrs C A Duffy, J P; P O'Connell, M J H
J P; Peter woods, J P; J P Kearney, wm Woods,
J P ; and James Wynne, J P.
Mr J M C Lyons, acting County Surveyor, and Lhe Harbour
Master, John Fearon, were also present.
The Committee inspected the Pier and found that the shed,
as reported by the Harbour Constable, was completely de-
molished, and all the contents, including the balance of a cask
of petroleum oil, destroyed.
The Committee consulted as to the advisa bility of erecting
a proper store on the Quay, which would .serve thr purpos«s of
the Council as a storeroom, and be jointly used by merchants
and importers in connection with the trade of the port, and
increase the revenue by inducing imports of coal to the
harbour for distribution in Carlingford and vicinity.
After discussion of the whole matter, the Committee were
practically unanimous in deciding that the Finance Committee
88
should be asked to recommend the County Council €0 erect
suitable store on Carlingford Quay for the regular storage of
all imports and exports, with a partitioned portion for €1:e
usage of the Council for storage of lamps, oil and other
articles.
The Committee desire to point out Chat thore is a sum cf
over to the credit of Carlingford Harbour account.
The only matter about wllidh a difference o! opinion exists is
whether the store, when erected, would be let any individual
which would give him practically a monopoly of the trade, or
whether it should be let as required to any person requiring it,
on the payment of a fixed -seale of charges, graded according to
the length of time it would be used.
After discussion, the Committee recommended the, County
Council €0 have a proper store erected on the Quay at Carling-
ford, with a partitioned portion for a storeroom for lamps, oil,
ete, for usage solely by the Council's officers, and a proper
W.C. under the same roof. The Committee suggest that 1110
store be not let to any person solely, but that everyone
requiring it pay whatever scale of Oharges be fixed for the term
so used, either at a tonnage rate or on time, and that higher
scale would be desirable where a user of the store held it after
a specified time.
MOTOR LICENSE DUTY AND ROAD MAINTENANCE.
The following letter was read and initialled by the Chair-
jnan : —
Eastgate, Castlebellingham,
co Louth, 30-3-'14.
The Secretary, Louth County Council.
Si r.
In reply to your letter re Motor Tas, I beg 'eo inform yon
that do not intendto pay any further Tax in Co Louth (but
to follow the example of other motor owners in this district,
and to do so elsewhere) until there is some evidences of con-
sideration being shown to the requirements and convenience
of motorists by the roads beipg put into a satisfactory con-
Gi tion.
It rnyust not be forgotten that owners of 1110tor cars put a
Very considerable amount into circulation in the county in the
way of wages and other rpotoring expenses, ancl they are fully
justified in expecting that the roads of County Louth should
at least equal those of the adjoining counties of Armagh,
Meath and Down.
Last year, on the strength of an assurance tnat great im-
provement was to be made very shortly; I paid the tax locally.
Since then matters have gone from bad to worse, and I have
therefore, with great regret, come to the decision I have above
in€imatecl,
I am, dear Sir,
Yours faithfully,
J. St. C. MACMILLAN.

SPEED LIMIT OF MOTORS IN COUNTY LOUTH.
Tho' following letter from Mi• O'Connell, ,Solici€or, was read
but no order was made
Francis Street, Dundalk,
11th April, 1914.
Dear Sir.
I beg to report that I havé been in communication with the
Local Government Board on the matter of tbe request of the,
Council, with a view to the public safety, that the Board should
make regulations limiting the driving of motor cars and motor
bicycles to a speed of ten miles an hour in the Urban Districts
of Dundalk, and Drogheda, and in Ardee, Carlingford, Omeafh,
Dunleer and Castlebellingham.
The Board state that they cannot make general regulations
of the nature referred t,o. That the case of each town or
village a speed limit for mot r traffic is desired must be
considered on its merits, having regard to the extent •and
character of the traffic, the width of the road, and the
position and number of bye-roads leading to the main streets.
Again, that they require a statement as to Why Section I of
the Motor Car Act, 1903, will not suffice to ensure the safety"
Of the public without any ordor béing made uncler Section 9.
Section 1 referred €0 has reference to the fact that it is an
offence to drive amotor car recklessly or negligently, or at a
speed or in a manner which is dangerous to the public; and
that a police constable may apprehend without warrant the
driver of any ear who commits an offence, etc. Section 9
deals with the power to have Regulations made, as proposed.
Certain information as hereunder sei, out is required by the
Board in connection with any application for regulations with
92
regard to specified hikhways. It will also be necessary for the
Council to give public notice of .sueh application by the inser-
tion of advertisements in the newspapers circulating in the
County. The Board intomate they will be prepared to consi-
der atty application. submitted' by the Council uncler these con-
ditions.
Tho information required ,and which I suggest may be sub-
No. A. 7271114.
plied by the County Surveyor, is as follows
Sir,.
1.
An ordnance map of the area showing the streets to be
affected.
2. A statement specifying the streets included in the appli-
cation, giving the width of each street and of the carriage way
and the gradient thereof.
3. A precise statement of the grounds on which the applica-
tion with regard to each street is based, with particulars of any
accidents which have occurred from the driving of motor cars
therein, and infornjation as to the extent of motor ear trafhe in
each such street.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
Charles M •Aiester, Esq,
Secretary I-outh County Council, I)uutla.lk.

inade of ()meath Park as well as an inspection of the plantation

ROAD TO DUNDALK RACE COURSE.
T wetter was read from Mr T Callan Marardle, J P, on behalf of
the Comtnittee of the Dundalk Raees, as follows :—
Dundalk, 14th April, 1914.
(Ili arles M'Alester, Esq,
Secretary Louth County Council,
Courthouse, Dundalk.
Dear Sir,—
I have not yet had the pleasure of' meeting our new County
Surveyor, but I would like you to ask him if he would mind,
or, still better, if you can spare the time fo go out with him, to
inspect the road leading from fhe County Road over to the Raee
Comse. Mr Gradwell, of Drogheda, is now our Secretary, and
the members of the Race Committee all think it would be a great
improvement if the grass edge along the road could be cleaned
away. It would make file road wider and safer for passengers.
A good many ladies object coming to Dundalk Raees on account
of the trouble with cars, and particularly the risk with so many
1110tors, aud if the County Surveyor would undertake to make
the improvements we could put it in our advertisement.
I am sure Mr Walsh will be glad to help us in every way, as
the Raees mean a big eireulåbtion of money in the district.
Yours faithfully,
T. CALLAN MACARDIÆ.
The, County Surveyor stated Mr Lyons was making some
improvements to this road, and he would inspect same at once
and see what could be done.

USAGE OF UNION FEVER HOSPITALS FOR PATIENTS
UNDER COUNTY TUBERCULOSIS SCHEME.
'Ille following letter from the Local Government Board wats
read and referred to tho Tuberculosis Committee of Manage-
mont for their consideration and : —
No. 71: M.
1914.
Miscellftneous.
Local Goveå•hment Board,
Dublin; 24th April, 1914.
Sir,
I am directed by [he Local Government Board for
fi•olåncl Ståité that théir attention has been frequently drawn
to the urgent nece—ity of iliaking ifroviS10n for the prSdpéi• iSO-
Iåtioji (Yf ffdvaneed eases of luberculOSiS so as to bi•event the
spread of i'nfee€ion from sueh eases.
21
it is generally recognised that no scheme of administrative
h etion which aims at the prevention and abolition of tuberculo-
sis can be Oompletely effective unless active steps are taken to
remove from small and crowded houses patients in an advanced
stagg of the disease. These patients are a constant source of
danger to the other inmates of the dwelling, and their rernoval
to a hospital, they eould receive medical attenclanee and
nursing, Would be in the best interests of the patients, their
friends Aiicl the public.
At the same time it is well known that patients in an ad-
Vanceci stage of Che disease will not, willingly go a hcspital
ni any eonsider:vble distanee from t!heir home, as their friends
and relatives could not have ready access to them. This, in
itself, constitutes one of the greatest difficulties in dealing with
the problem. To suggest the setting up of new institutions
for the purpose all over the country would, at least in some
counties, jmeet with opposition on finanmal and administrative
The fever hospitals in England, Wales and Scotland aro
usually called isolation hospitals, and many Of them provi-
Shon has been made for the treatment Of patients sufferi21g
from tuberculosis. These hospitals are administered by the
sanitary authorities, ancl as they are not poor law institutions
there is no barrier to their being titijised for the treatment of
insured persons suffering from tubercu104s.
The Board have given this question most caretul considera-
tion with a view to making a recommendation which they trust
will solve the difficulty without creating new institutions. They
are of opinion that a number of the present Union fever hos-
Ditals might be transferred to the District Councils where the
latter body are the sanitary authority for the entire Union acrea
served by the hospital,
22
The name, District, Isolation Ilospital, might •be substituted
for that of Union Fever Hospital. The hospital would also bg
available for the reception and treatment of patientß suffering
from tuberculosis •by adding two pavilions or chalefg for
purpose, one for male and one for female patients.
If this recommendation meets witih the approval of the bo-
dies concerned it will have the advantage of economy in t}he
capitol cost and in the cost of administration. It would solve
the innumerable difficulties connected with the acquisition of
sites for hospitals for tuberculous patients.
Tho hospitals might also be co-ordinated with the County
scheme for the treatment of tuberculosis 'by arrangement . be.
tween the County Council and the District Council concerned
on the basis of a contribution to be fixed between those bodies.
By these means a considerable relief of tho poor rate might be
effected, seeing that whreas tho cost of institutional treatment,
of poor persons suffering from tuberculosis is now borno in full
by the Union rates, the corresponding charge under the ar-
rangements above indicated would rank for recoupment from
the Exchequer Grant in aid of tuberculosis. It will thus 'be ob-
vious that an arrangement on the lines suggested will be to the
advantage of the local and County ratepayers, and, at the same
tine, will have a important and far-reaching effect upon
tho eradication of tuberculosis.
I am, Sira
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARLAS,
Seeretnrv,
The Secretary
of each County Councål.

amount of it would be so small as to be
The Secretary was directed to reply accordingly to Che Board

NITENDANCES AT COUNTY COMMITTEE MEETINGS.
Tho following circular letter was read and initialled by the
(„"hairman :—
Department of Agricnllfure and Teclm,ieal I histruetion for Ireland,
Upper Merrion Street,
Dublin,
8th May, 1914.
No. 12332—14.
Sir,—
T have to acquaint you, for Che inforniation of your
Gouncil, that the Department have had before them returns of the
number of attendances of members of County Committees of
Agriculture at meetings held during the past year
Reviewing the figtnos for the county as a. whole, it is observed
that the average attendance per mesoting slightly exceeds twenty
per cent of the total n umber of members. This very low average
suggests the inference that persons are appointed on these Com-
mittees who either do not take sv.fficient interest in or are unable
to devote the necessary time to the businc» of the Committees ;
and Chis inference is borne out by the fact that. in the case of
several Committees a considerable number of the members did
not. attend a single meeting during the six months ended March
last.
Save in very exceptional circumstances the retention on a Com-
iii\ttee of members who take no rctive participation in the work
of the Committee is, in the view of the Department, undesirable.
'It a ecrtain amount of expense €0 the Committee
considerable trouble to its Seeretary, which might be avoided.
Moreover, even though if does not; always interfere with the
efficiency of the Committee's work, it cannot but convey an un-
favourable impression w11iclJ is unfair to those members who {Vt-
tend the meetings i•egolarly.
'The existing County Committee will go out of office at the same
•time •as the County Councils which appointed them, and, there-
fore, when the new County Councils are elected in June next,
•one of timeir duties will be to appoint Committees for the
purposes of the Agriculture and Technical Instruction (Irelmnd)
Act, 1899. Accordingly, viellV of whaf has been stated above,
"the Department would strongly urge upon County Councils when
appointing the new Committeexs the desirability of making the
appointment subject to the condition that any member of the
Committee who is absent from meetings of the Committee during
period of six consecutive months shall, ipso facto, cease to be
member of the Committee, and his office shall thereupon be-
•come vaeants
1 am, Sir,
Your obedient
T. P GILL,
SeeretaVS
Of each County

ORDER OF TELE DEPARTMENT OF AGRICULTURE AND
TECHNICAL INSTRUCTION FOR IRELAND.
(Datcd 21st April, 1914.)
THE MOVEMENT OF ANIMALS (SOUTHERN IRELAND)
TEMPORARY PROHIBITION ORDER (No. 3).
The Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction
for Ireland, by virtue and in exercise of the powers vested in
them under the Agriculture and Technical Instruction (Ireland)
Act, 1899, the Diseases of Animals Acts, 1894 to 1911, and of
every other power enabling them in this behalf, do order, ancl
it is hereby ordered, as follows
Alteration of Scheduled Area.
1. On and after the twenty-second day of April, nineteen
hundred and fourteen, 11%e Movement of Animals (Southern
Ireland) Temporary Prohibition Order shall be read as if the
Area described in the Schedule to that Order were the Area
described in the First Schedule to this Order.
Order Revoked.
2. The Order described in the Second Schedule to this
Order is hereby revoked from the twenty-second day of April,
nineteen hundred and fourteen, inclusive.
Short Title.
3. This Order may be cited as The Movement of Animals
46
(Southern Ireland) Temporary Prohibition Order (No. 3).
In witness whereof the Department of
and Technical Instruction for Ireland have hero-
unto set their Official Seal this twenty-first day of
April, nineteen hundred and fourteen.
(Signed) T. P. GILL,
Secretary
FIRST SCHEDULE.
Scheduled Area.
So much of Ireland as lies to the south of the following
boundary (roads and railway lines mentioned as forming the
boundary, except the Great Southern and Western Railway
Line from Clara to Banagher, Ining as outside the
Scheduled Area) :—
Commencing at the Maiden Tower, 011 tho sea coast at
Mornington, thence by the course of the River Boyne to the
Railway Fridge at Drogheda, thence by tfhe Great Northern
Railway Line €0 its junction at Navan with the Midland
Western Line, thence southwards by the Midland Great,
Western Line to Kilmessan Station, thence westwards 'by the
Athboy Branch of that Line as far as the point where it
crosses the load from Navan to Trim at Peterstown, thence
southwards by the road via Newtown to the junction with the
road from Scurlockstown Bridge to Trim, thence by that road
through Trim to Roristown as far us the iunction with Che
bye-road over Derringdaly Bridge, thence by that road to tho
junction with the road from Ballivor to Trim, thence by that
road to Ballivor, thence by the direct road via Raharney to
Killucan, thence by the direct road from Killucan via Kil-
47
iuean Railway Station to the junction uGth the main road
loading from Kinnegacl to Mullingar, thence by the main road
via Rochforibriclge, Tyrrellspa.ss and Kilbeggan to the Mid-

Line to its junetion w it'll the Great Southern and Western
Line south of Clara, thence by that Line to Banagher Station,
thence by the direct road to the River Shannon, and thence
by the course of that river to t,he sea.

It should be remembered when considering the proposed site

Letter No. 71 AI, 24th April, 1914, fronj T, G B, was
submitted, suggesting the use of existing Vevet• Hospitals
treating chronic advanced of Tuberculosis.
After very careful consideration and discussion, it
unanimously derided €0 strongly recommend and urge on
and
first
f or
was
the
53
County Council to aet as far as possible on the suggestions con-
tairrvcl in this letter from the I, G B, as we are satisfied, if carried
out and co-ordinated with the Council's Scheme for the treatment
of Tuberculosis, will be found to be to the general advantage and
tend towards :—
1st Economy—As the cost of erecting male and female pavilions
in the vicinity of the District TTospitals (as it is suggested to namo
them) will be a. comparatively small outlay of Capital Expenditure,
which will be. borne by T"reasury Grant.
2nd Efficiency—As stated in the letter in question, no scheme
is complete. until proper provision is made to treat. chronic cases
of Tubereulosis, reducing the dangers of infection to a, minimum
by isolation. Experience already teaches there are no institutions
available for such eases, and that providing Shelters for such pur-
poses is not practicable, espeeially in urban areas, while, under tho
suggested arrangement, it is obvious there is a possibility of
having, for small outlay, three centres in Louth for advanced
eases, namely, Dundalk, Drogheda and Ardee.
The (hnmittee desive to add that they are confident that. Poor
Law Authorities, close examination of the suggested arrange-
ntents, will lurve little hesitation to facilitate the Council fo carry
through, especially as destitute persons will be treated under
the Council's Scheme, and half the cost will be borne by tho
Imperial Fund, of considerable advantage fo tho
•ratepayers.
Letter 48 M, 1914, dated 6th Mayell, 1914, from the L G B, was
also submitted and considered.
Tt was decided to draw particular attention to the last threo
paragraphs, and to state, it is the unanimous opinion of this
Committee that the suggestions contained in this letter, if found
practicable, decides the contentious question of the erection of
costly Dispensaries.
We therefore desire to recommend the County Council fo direct
that, negotiations be opened with the Managing Body of the Louth
County Infirmary, with a view to having a wing added to the
Institution, or pavilions erected in the grounds attached, or both,
where beds for observation purp«ses migh,t be erected. We also
approve of and recommend tho suggestion to provide a ward for
children suffering flom non-pulmonary tubtn•eulosis.
Ln these cases also the cost will be defrayed by the Treasury
Grant.

mat on Fridåiy last at the County Offices, and, witer consulta-

the Sheep-Seab Order of 1914 should be made v the County
Council, and that the Solicitor should draft the Peg-ulations :
(a)
SHEEP DIPPING ORDER.
COUNTY LOUTH, 1914.
That the owner or person tooving Sheep from outsido
County Louth into said County be required to notify
forthwith the arrival of the Sheep to Constable or
other member of the Police Foree at the nearest Pcliee
Station, who shall forthwith transmit t'he information
to the Secretary of the Louth County Council.
4
(b) All such Sheep moved , into tho County Lout.)) shall
be isolated until inspection and dippi •g has been
completed to €110 satisfaction of the Local Authority,
and IWIieh dipping must be carried out within Séven
(7) days from the date on which the Sheep have been
moved into the County.
Nothing in this Order shali apply to. Sheep ved into the
County Louth for immediate shipment or slaughter.

VETERINARY INSPECTORS' SALARIES.
By consent of the Council this matter was taken out of its
•order on the agenda. The Secretary read the letters from the
Department sanctioning the increases in the salaries of the
three Veterinary Inspectors, Messrs Clinton, Donnelly, and
Maxwell, and as tlhe term of these increases expired on the
1st June, 1914, they now came up for consideration by the
•County Council.
After hearing Mr Clinton, V S, and Mr Maxwell, V S, in
connection with the work under the Tuberculosis Order (Ire-
land) of 1913, and after discussion on the whole matter, the
following resolution was unanimously passed
by Mr Michael O'Meara, J P,
Seconded by Mr Joseph P Kearney, and
Resolved—That, the increased remuneration payable to the
Veterinary Inspectors, Messrs Clinton, Donnelly, and Max-
well, in respect of the e«tra work under the Tuberculosis (Ire—
land) Order of 1913, be reduced by the sum of CIO (ten pounds)
in eacqh ease, and that at the end of one year from the 1st day
of June, 1914, tho rates of salary be reviewed in the light of
the work under tho Tuberculosis Order that may have been
•earried out during the year by the Inspectors.
This makes the salaries as follows T Clinton, C120, A A
Donnelly, C80, and W E Maxwell, C80 per annum respectively.

IRISH VOLUNTEERS.
Tho following resolution submitted by the
Council, was adopted :—
Proposed by Mr Edward Goodman ;
Seconded by Mr Miehåel Meade, and
Limerick County
Resolved—That owing to recent political events, and parti-
eularly to the attitude of the army on the Ulstei• question, we,
the members of the Louth County Council, consider that the
time has come when effort should be made to organise the Irish
Volunteer Force in every parish in Ireland. We are convinced
that such a force, wisely led by men in wilorn our indomitab.te
leader, Mr J E Redmond, would have confidence, would prove
to the world that the manhood of Ireland are ready to do their
part, in defence of their homes and their liberties, and if need
to protect, our fellow-Nationalists in tjlster from insult and
attack.

IMPORTATION OF HAY A-ND STRAW.
The following resolution submitted by the Wicklow County
Council, was adopted unanimously
Proposed by Mr Michael Meade ;
Seconded by Mr Patrick Donegan, J P, and
Resolved—That this Council protests against the revocation
of tome Order prohibiting the importation of Hay and Straw
into this country from Great Britain, and we strongly rurge„
11
that suitable measures should be adopted to prevent the impor—
tation of Hay and Straw, either when used as packing, or in
any other form: as we beiieve the Foot and Mouth Disease has
been introduced into this country by this means.
ATTENDANCE OF JEY{ORS AT ASSIZES.
A circular letter from the Belfast Corporation was read p
pointing out the very inconvenience caused to Jurors
summohed to- Asusizes, by being delayed from day to day, and
asking if the Louth County Council would co-operate with tiherrv
with a view to having such an alteration in the law made as
would have the conditions at all Assizes in Ireland the same as
applies to County Cork, where the attendance of Jurors is ar-
ranged for separate days.
It was unanimously decided to co-operate with the Belfast
Corporation in any action taken to make the ehanges sugges-
ted.

following letter from Mr Patr?ck J. Roe, Ret.orning
Officer, was read, also letter on the same subject from the Lo-
eal Government Board :—
Fvancis Street,
Dundalk, 11th June, 1914.
Local Government Elections, 1914.
Co. Louth Ardee No. I Rural District.
Dear Sir,—
The two retiring Rural District Councillors for
the District Electoral Division of Drumcar, whiOh elects two
Councillors, were John Carroll Harmon, of •Salterstown, and
Joseph Sy!vestor Harmon, of Salterstown. Both these men
were duly nominated for re-election, and no otihem• nomination
avas received for the division. •On the 7th May I issued a no-
tice of No Poll, stating that these two eonåida.tes would 'be de-
clared to be elected as Rüral District Councillors for the divi-
Sion of Drumcar. On the 15th May John Carroll Harmon
died. The day of election was the 4th June. John 'Carroll
Harmon being the retiring councillor there was one Whom I
could declare elected to his seat, and I issued notice of the re-
sult of elections, declaring Joseph Sylvester Harmon duly elec-
ted. The notice is silent as to the other seat.
T wrote Che Local Government Board for directions, and they
referred me to Article 7 (1) of the Schedule to the Local Go-
•vernment (Application of Enactments) Order, 1898, which pro-
sides if any difficulty arises as respects thc election of any in-
dividual Councillor, Alderman, Guardian or Town Commis-
25
•ioner, and there is 110 provision for 'holding another election
then, in the case of a County Councillor or of an Alderman of
. a County Borough, Che Local Government Board, and in any
other case the County Council may order a new election €0 be
held, and may give such directions as may be necessary for the
purpose of holding the election.
In their reply to my letter Local Government Board state
the matter is one for the County Council and not for the Board
to determine.
T would be glad if you would bring Chis matter under the no-
tice of your Council at the earliest opportunity, and I await
their directions.
Yours faithfully,
PATRICK J. ROE.
•Charles M' Alester, Esq,
Secretary Louth County Council,
Dundalk.

TILLAGE RETURNS.
Mr Patrick M 'Gee broubht under the notice of the Council
the question of the action of the Department of Agriculture in
publishing the tillage returns of the various counties in Ireland
i nthe early part of the Autumn, which gave the brewers and
distillers an opportunity of making a basis of Ehe price of grain,
which was prejudicial €0 the farmers of the County Louth, and
suggested that the Department be solicited to withhold the re-
turns until the 1st of November in each year.
3
After •discussion the following resolution was passed
mously : —
Proposed by Patrick M •Gee, Esq. seconded by Michael Moade
Esq, and
Resolved—That in view of the fact that the early publica-.
lion of the tillage returns by the Department of Agriculture, is..
prejudicial to t.he farmers •by enabling brewers and distillers
fix a standard price for grain, before the actual yield is defini-
tely known, we request the Department to withhold the publi-
cation of the tillage returns until the 1st Novem bev in each
year.

Local Government Board, Dublin,
19th June, 1914.
No. 31483/1914.
Louth County.
Sir ,
The Local Government Board for Ireland have had before
them your letter of the 10th instant, and previous correspondeneo
relative to the payment of poundage fees to the Poor Rate Col-
lectors in County Louth for the past financial year; and with
reference thereto, T am to state that the replies received from
the Collectors are not satisfactory, ancl in the circumstances, the
Board can only feel justified in consenting to the payment of
31
remuneration to them as follows in respect of last year's collec-
tion :—
(a) Tn respect of the first moie«ty, poundage fees on amount
. aetually lodged to the credit of the Council on 30th September,
and in the case of Collectors who aro paid by salary, a propor-
fion of half year's salary on amount corresponding to amount
lodged on that date.
(b) Full remuneration in resper•t of second moiety, of which
•enfire amount was lodged on 31st March.
The Board will •be prepared to consider the payment of poundage
fees of the 1st moiety at close of thet current half year's collec-
-tion„ but only so in the ease Of those Collectors who make sub-
stantial lodgment during early parf of half year, as indicated
by the Board's letter of the 27th ultima, and lodge full amount
Of 1st moiety by 30th September.
The Board will now object to Collector 11 uglies being paid
his poundage fees in reyspe,et of the year endca March, 1913, as in
the case of the other Collectors.
1 am, Sir,
Your obedient Sérvaut,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
The Secretary Louth Co. Council
Courthouse, Dundalk.

day, the 24th instant, and to explain its contents and discuss the
t€mns of [Ime letter and have their views on tho matter.

COSTS OF LEASE OF SITE OF TUBERCULOSIS
DJSPENSARY.
The following letter from Mr O'Connell, Solicitor, was read,.
and after discussion tho Committee Ieeommencled that the ac—
count for the costs be paid:—
Fraricis Street, Duudalk,
28th May, 1914.
Earl of Roden with County Council.
Re Lease of Site on Castleföwn Road, Dundalk.
Dear Sir,—
I have your letter of 26th inst, enclosing costs of pro-
of Lease as received from the Lessor's Solicitors.
34
The costs of a Lease are always borne by Che Lessee, and in
instance this draft Lease was prepared at our request and
ordinarily the Council would remain liable whether the Lease
was sealed and completed or cancelled befimy completion.
The only point that arises is as to the cancellation. 1 fear we
would have much difficulty in, proving before a Court that the
Lessor had, after draft Lease had been approved of and en-
grossed and awaited completion, contracted with the Urban Dis-
trict Council for the granting of a Lease of a plot of ground
which included some portion of the plot included in our draft
In all the circumstances I considtr the Council would be held
liable, and I advise payment. 1 return costs heerwith.
Yours fai{hfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
Charles M 'Alester, Esq,
Secretary, Louth Co. Council, Dundalk.
FORESTRY SCHEME.
OMEATFI PARK, RUXTON DEMESNE, AND WILLISTOWN
PLANTATIONS.
The following letter from the Department of Agriculture was
read, also report on Omeath Park, the Ruxton Demesne, and
Nillistown Plantations:—
Department of Agrieultur€x and Technical Instruction for
J reland,
Upper Merrion Street, Dublin,
27th May, 1914.
No. A. 11827-14.
FORESTRY.
Sir,—
Adverting to pl+vious correspondence, T have now to
transmit, for the, information of Louth County Council, copy
•of the report furnished to the Department by their Forestry
Inspector, Mr John Crozier, on his inspectic;n of the lands and
*timber offcu•ed for sale to the Council a € Omeatll Park. If; is
•observed that the area. available for purchase on this Estate has
now been reduced €0 about eighty-four acres. If this property,
including the timber now standing thereon, can be secured by
•the Council under the Land Purchase Acts at a price not ex-
•eeeding 81,000, the Department will be prepared to sanction its
.aequisition, under the conditions of a, County Forestry Scheme
adopted by the Council on the lines already suggested by the
Department.
A copy Of the report of the Department's Chief Forestry In-
spector, regarding the plantation on the Ruxon Estate at Ardoe,
and on the Rathdonnell Estate at Willistown, which the Council
propose to purchase, is also sent herewith.
The Department note from your letter of Che 4th ultimo, that
no definite steps have yet been taken by the Council in regard
to the purchase of the Woodlands at Ardee. The Department
will be prepared to consider favourably the question of the ac-
equisition of this property at a reasonable price under au ap-
proved County Forestry Scheme, in the event of the Council de-
eiding to proceed with the purchase.

REPORT AND VALUATION OF TIMBER ON LAND OFFERED*
FOR SALE AT OMEATH PARK, COUNTY LOUTH.
The total area of land offered for sale on the above property
extends to 84 acres O roods 24 perches, but of that total not morep
than fifty acres are stocked or partially stocked with timber,-
the remainder consisting of grass land, ground Of plarv
tation, bare patches within the woods, etc.
The plantations have been laid out with a view to creating
ornament and shelter around the mansion house ruther than
for the purpose Of utility, and, although occupying a fairly come.
pact block of land and sheltered by the contour of the ground
from their prevailing winds, their composition, and the methods..
Of thinning practised ha.ve largely robbed tllé area Of any
sibility of acquiring a value wheh, under better seloction Ofl
economical timber trees and moro approved methods of thnningv_
it: would undoubtedly have' attained. The land is well adapted'
for the• cultivation of coniferous trees, and good examples are too
be seen of Scots pine, larch, Douglas fir, Pinus insignis, and
certain spocies of Silver fir, but the soil, with the exception of
a small area on the low ground, is too dry and light for the»•
growth of hardwoods, and the extensive planting of these which
has taken place within the last fifty years must inevitably lead?
to financial failure.
A considerable amount of clearing and thinning out has taken,
place within the last few years, and is at present, or has latelyu
been, proceeding; whilst the best . of the pitwood, larch, and a
certain amount of Scots pine wnd hardwoods. have also been
marked for sale.
The growing stock now consist of about thirty-five acres of
timber of from thirty-five to fifty years of age, largely consist-
ing of a mixture of hardwoods and conifers, with two smalV
groups Of pure Iwrch, covering au area of about three acr€s, and
a larger area on the noth-west side of practically pure hardwoods..
together with about fifteen acres partiall} stocked with matured
Scots pine, throughout which a few larches of equal age are to
37
-seen. Both young and old woods have been. depleted to a large
•extent of the Iapch with which they were originally plantod in
mixture, the steady demand and satisfactory prices offered for
pitwood apparently contributing to this end. Larch suitable
for pitwood locally fetches from 14s to 15s per ton, ash 18s to
6d, alder 7s 6d to IOS; while Scots pine and spruce are in
little demand, and average from 5s to 7s per ton, all these timbers
being sold standing.
Tn the event' Of purchase by t.hé County Council taking place,
-sum of about 2150 might be realised by the sale of the clumps
•of pitwood larch, • and the old Scots pines on the higher ground
' towards the south-west boundary, and in the subsequent workinÅ
of the area it would be necessary, in order to make full use of
tho land, to clear Off a considerable amount Of immature timber.
With the exception of the larch clumps above referred to, and
•an area of seven acres or so in the centre of the main block, the
remainder of the area. is much under-stocked, and the hope Of
future profit still further lessened by the fact that most of tho
trees left are not suitable for the soil, or of u kind likely to
yield satisfactory financial results.
In computing the value, the present. worth of the under-
"stocked areas must be taken as a basis of calculation, and, making
due allowance for the cost of planting and maintenance of
fully stocked areas, I estimate that sum of from Æ400 to 8425
represents the full value of the timber offered for sale on, the
property•
(Signed), JOHN CROZIER.
24th April, 1914.
No. A. 11827-14.
REPORT ON WOODS ON THE ARDEE DEMESNE, COUNTY
LOUTH.
These Woods were inspected on the 11th and 12th May. They
«jnsist of about sixty aeres on. the north and south sides of the
Ardee Demesne—thirty-eight acres lying in the Lawn and Mount
Woods, and about twenty acres in the Foxcover.
38
'The Lawn Wood contains a considerable quantity of very OIN
arul ornamental timber, with a lurge stock of seedlings and young-
trees coming up between. This Wood is bouded on the Southy
East, and West sides by a wall, while the North side is more,
or less open to the demesne, and could be fenced off without'
great difficulty.
The Foxcover contains younger wood, chiefly beech, sycamore,-
ash, and a few conifers, and has been planted at two different
periods. The younger portion of this wood earries a very
promising erop which should be left entirely alone for some
years, but tho older portion could be cleared out an,d replanted.
at any time.
The fences round the Foxcover are fairly good,
although a few gaps occur along the roadside. There are various.
signs of pilfering having taken place lately.
The total area of thesa Woods, amounting to sixty acres, would
qualify them for an advance from the Development Fund, and
their position near the town of Arcleo gives them considerable-
importance in the dstriet.
The older wood on the south side of the Demesno is extremely
picturesque, and it would be difficult to treat it on economic
lines without spoiling its pr«sent appearance; and robbing it of
one of its most attractive feutures. Tn the event of its acquisi-„
tion by the County Council, special treatment might be prcv
vided for with regard to this wood. The woods to the u.ortll,.
on the other hand, could be dealt with in the ordinary way by
clearing and replanting the older portion and leaving thev younger,
wood to mature. ground on which both woods stand is
exceptionally good, and capable of producing first-class timber,
and many of the specimens now present ns good as any existing
in Ireland.
On the whole, these woods appear particularly suitable for a.
County Council Scheme, owing to their well-fenced condition,
their position as regards the district generally, and their
proximity to the town and railway of Ardee. The value of the
standing timber was not ascertained with any degree of accuracy y.
but would probably be worth from E6C0 to 8800 if sold standing,
39
WILLISTOWN.
Two small plantations in Willistown consist of a mixod crop
of larch, Scots pine, spruce, silver fir, sycamore, ash, and oak
of about twenty years of •age. These woods are in very promis-
ing condition, and should • produce good timber in tho course
of thirty or forty years. At the present moment nothing re—
quires doing, except a few repairs to ff'nces on the west side of
one of the woods, the fences generally being in a good con-
dition.
Owing to their distance from any large centre, and their small
size, I do not consider these woods suitable for a County Council
Scheme, although exceptional ciscumstanees might render their
purchase desirable, such as the possibility of additional woods
being acquired within a short distance. They are, however, well
worth the 275 paid for them, and I recommend that the sanction
of tho Department be given to this transaction.
(Signed), A. C. FORBES.
18th May, 1914.

Dundalk, 29th June, 1914.
Monthly Report.
Sir,—
I beg to report that a case of suspected swine fever oc--
curred on the premises of Mr P Murphy, Blackrock. I visited
the place in company with au Inspector from the Department,
and the necessary steps have been taken ill the matter. A case
of bovine tuberculosis occurred in a cow the property of P-
M 'Gorman, Thornfield. The animal was slaughtered in accord-.
ance with the Tuberculosis (Ireland) Order and the carcase,
buried.
With reference to your communication re sheep dipping in
Ravensdale, Carlingford, ancl adjoining areas, I would respectfully
point out that as matters are at present the Order is practically
useless. There is no supervision, and most of the owners goe
througli a form of dipping at their own. premises which is littl&
short of a farce.
Your Obedient Servant,
J. CLINTON.

-with the matter :—
Francis Street, Dundnlk,
2nd July, 1914.
Dear Sir,
I have your letter of 1st instant, enclosing ac-
count now furnished by the Town Clerk of Drogheda of fees for
work done and payments made with respect' to the election of
County Councillors and Guardians for the Borough of Drogheda
in the year 1911.
The account should have been furnished to the then Return-
ing Officer "within fotllteen days after the day on which the
return is made of the person or persons elected at the election."
It is Obvious this has not been done, and the then returning
officer was freed' from liability for payment. I am of opinion
that the Council cannot pay the amount without being liable
to a surcharge. If it is desired to effect settlement, then the
consent of the Loead Government, Board must first be had, and
the account be approved in detail 'by Che County as being in
with the scale of expenses applicable.
Yours faithfully,
(Sgd.) DANIEL O'CONNELL.

BYE-LAWS WITH RESPECT TO INDECENT EXPRES-

The following letter enclosing the acompanying bye-laws, was
read, and the Secretary was directed €0 send copy to the Lord
Lieutenani when ready :—
Francis Street, Dundalk,
2nd July, 1914.
Re Bye-Laws with Respect to the Use of Indecent
Expressions and Conduct.
Dear Sir,
I enclose clraft of Bye-Laws as approved and set-
•led by Counsel. It is considered that the clause giving power
io a Constable to arrest should not be inserted as the law is
somewhat unsettled in the case of misdemeanors. There can be
no arrest withoutwanrant, made, unless the abusive language or
•eonduet constitutes a. •breach of the peace, and, in the latter
event, the arrest can be made without a warrant at Common
Law.
The Bye-Laws hould come into •operation on the espiration of
forty days after a copy has bee nsen€ to the Lord Lieutonant,
,and if they are not, within t;hat time, disallowed by him with
the advice of the Privy Council.
I would direct your attention €0 the notes in Vanston's
-eal Government, Vol. 1, page 19, to Section 16 of the Local
Government (Ireland) Act, 1898, as to procedure as regards tho
attendance of at least two-thirds of the whole number of the
68
Council at the making of the bye-law,
a copy in some public place.
Yours faithfully,
the sealing, and affixing

Bye-Laws with Respect to the Use of Indecent Expressions
and Conduct.
I.—No person shall in any street or public place or on land
adjoining or near to such street, make use of any abusive, prcv
fane, blasphemous, indecent, or obscene language, behaviour or
conduct , to the annoyance or disturbance of any person in.
such street or place.
2.—Any person acting contrary to the foregoing Bye-Law
shall be liable for every such offence to a fine of Forty Shillings
provided, nevertheless, that the Justices or Court before whom
any complaint may be made, or any proceedings may be taken
in respect of any such offence, may, if they think fit, adjudge
bho payment of a fine of any sum less than the full amount: of
the fine imposed by this Bye-Law.

day of

14.
Office of Public Works,
Dublin, 17th June, 1914.
In reply* to your letter of the 11th inst (31076-14)
relative to a proposal to hand over the management of the Fano
72
Drainage District to the Louth •County Council, I am directed
by the Commissioners of Public Works to state that the district,
was formed under the 5 and (j Vie. , cap. 89, at a cost of
0,155 15s (Sd, and was brought to award on the 20th Septem-
ber, 1851. The area of lands drained or improved was 1,522
acres. In accordance with Section 120 of the Act of 1842 men-
tioned above, the management of the District is vested in trus—
tees, who shall be elected triennially by the proprietors in the
District, but in; consequence of action taken by the Board, 15
trustees were appointed in July, 1900. Early in that year the
district was reported to be in need of maintenance, but from
a return appended to the Minutes of Evidence of the Arterial
Drainage Commission, 1905 (pp. 416-7) it appears that no ex-
was incurred in the maintenance of the Fane Drain-
age District from 1901 to 1905 inclusive, and since 1908 the
Board, in spite of repeated efforts, have been unable to ensure
the triennial election of trustes contemplated by Section 120 of
the 5 and G Vic. , cap. 89 (eopy enclosed).
The Board have no knowledge as €0 the present condition of
the district but presumably there is no systematic maintenance,
there are no acting trustees, and the former Secretary has cea-
sed to act as such. It appears, therefore, to the Board to 'be
desirable that the business of the district should be transferred
to the County Council.
am ,Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
H. WILLIAMS,
Secretary.
The Secretary,
Local overnment Board, Dublin.

APPLICATION FRO-MI DOMINICAN COLLEGE.
Letter was read from Sister Mary Reginald, Dominican Col-
lege, Dublin, asking that the rule as to t'he test examination
for Senior Grade Intermediate Examination, 1914, should Ibe
altered to enable students who had passed the Senior Grade Ex-
amination, 1913, €0 be eligible.
The Committee decided that as the scheme for the 1914
Scholarships made a pass in the 1914 Intermediate Senior Grade
a necessary condition, no change could be made.

Dominican Hall, Stephen's Green,

This is the third year of my tenure

gree of B. Comm. ; but my ability to do so depends entirely on

grees the same year, but .0Ming to the extent of t'he commerce
eourse it was impossible to do so.

Deatb of Mr. Peter Hughes, J.P.,

notice of the Committee the lamented death of Mr.

DEATH OF MR. PETER HUGHES, CHAIRMAN OF THE
LOUTH COUNTY COUNCL.
The following correspondence was read :—
Rosmakea,
Dundalk, 23rd July, 1914.
To the Presiding Chairman Of the Louth County C 'cuncil,
County Council Offices, Courthouse, Dundalk
Dear Sir,—
I regret that owing to indisposition I will be unable
to be present at the meeting to-day, but I assume that beyond
the transaction of the imperative business, the Council will ad-
journ for at least a week„ indeed I should suggest their doing
so until our meeting in August.
I cannot say how sorry I am to be .unable to join publiely to-day
in tho general regret of the passing away of Peter Hughes, rny
old friend and faithful colleague. He is gone indeed from am-
ongsb us, "gone where road boards cease from troubling and
A•ouneils are at rest." May God have mercy on his soul.
Yours faithfully,
THOMAS J. BYRNE.
the County-
4
Viceregal Lodge. Dublin.
Secretary Louth County Council, Courthouse, Dundalk.
Please convey to your Council the expresion of most sincere
sympathy in the loss of your Chairman, from the Lord Lieuten-
ant and myself, and also from the Peamount Committee of
Management.
LADY ABERDEEN.
House of Commons.
To M' Alester, Secretary County Council, Dundalk.
Have learned with deepest regret of death of Mr Peter
Hughes. Pray convey to his family tho expression of my sin--
cero sympathy.
JOHN TIEDMOND.
The Chairman then referred in feeling terms to the lamented'
death of Mr Hughes, whose connection with the County Coun-
eil dated from its establishment, and whose ability and know-
ledge had been its guiding influence to the present time. He
proposed the resolution as set out below, and this was seconded'
by Mr Edward Goodman and supported by Messrs IRath, Harn-
ill, J P, and T Murphy
Proposed by VCiIiam A Doran, Esq, J P;
Seconded by Edward Goodman, Esq, and
Resolved—That we learn witih heartfelt SOITOW of the death or
onr respcw•ted Chairman, Peter Hughes, E.sq, J P. Associated
a.s he was with the Louth County Council as Chairman since its
establishment in 1899, his lamented death has caused a void
which will not be easily filled. His thorough grasp of the in-.
tricate details of the work of the Council, his administrativ€y
ability, his taet and unfailing courtesy as Chairman, and,.
5
above all, his devotion to the interests of our county, and of
Ireland generally, has won our respect and admiration. We beg
to tender to 'his relatives our sincere condolence in the great
and irreparable loss they have sustained, and we adjourn this
meeting until Thursday next, the 30th day of July, as a mark
.of respect to his memory.
'Tho resolution• was passed in silence, all the members standing
as a mark of respect.
The Council cleeided that the co-option of a member to fill
the vaeancy, and the election of a Chairman of the Council,
should not be made until the second Quarterly Adjourned
Meeting on the 20th August.

at hand there, and that the arangement was merely temporary,
