FANE AND DRAINAGE msnucrs.
The following report from Mr Walsh, County Surveyor, was
tread : —
Wottonstown and Fane Drainage Districts
Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
I have made a preliminary inspection of the
•above-named drainage districts, and beg to report as follows
The Wottonstown District includee; approximately 7 miles of
•drains in the townlands of Cullenstown, Milltown Grange, Mill-
town, Dellin, Newtown Darver, Milltown Old, Whiterath,
Newrath, Babeswood, Mansfields;town, Wottonstown and Drom-
iskin. Part of the drainage goes to the river Glyde, and part
to the river Fane. The Glyde drainage system is, I under-
stand, attended to by Trustees.
The greater part of the water of Che Wottonstown District'
however, flou%S to the Fane river via one of its tributaries flow-
ing through Milltown and Commons, and included amongst
•the works of the Fane Drainage Districts, as shown by tho
•award maps.
11
The drainage works in this Wottonstown district were car-
Tied out in 1851, and have had little, if any, attention since. A
rough estimate of the expenditure now required would be C'25()
to C300, to out the drains in order and maintain for one
. year.
The total area of land improved and drained as Shown by
maps and award is 31Ca Ir 17 p. The total area of lands char-
ged and chargeable with the maintenance is 2473a. 3r 1 Ip.
The money expended on the drainage in 1851 amounted to
El 102 13s Ocl, of which C8() 17s 7d was presented by Che Grand
Jury of C.Ounty Louth, in of certain works in the dis-
.trict chargeable to the county.
The Fane Drainage M'orks include
(1)—The Tidal Embankment at Mooretown (or Marsh Farm)
.about 1} miles in length.
5} miles of river Fane (with embankments) from
the sea. to the western boundary of Stephenstown Demesne,
i.e., a noint cn the river about a mile above Stephenstown
Blidge.
Cb miles of important, tributaries with tidal
•sluices.
This drainage scheme was carried out, in 1851, also with Cho
•aid of a Board of Works Loan. The area of tho land drained
and improved is, according to the awwrcl, 1521a, 3r 2Sp. The
area charged with the works is 4736a 1 r 2p in the various town-
lands ,as shown in the award (which I beg to lay before you).
As jn the case of the Wotfonstown District, very little, if
•any, attention has 'been given €0 the drains since the works
Were first carried out. The main Fane river as far as its old
12
course forming the eastern boundary of Rossmakea, appears to,
be in fairly clean condition, with the exception of a sharp at
the Green Road Bridge after the river crosses the railway. The.
Rossmalwa and Stephenstown portion is more or less obstruc-
ted by weeds growing on the bottom of the course. Opposite
Mr Craig's house in Walterstown there are stepping stones and
a cattle watering place obstructing the flow. A hove Stephens-
town to the south-east of Stephenstown House, two weirs have
been erected some two years ago, I was told; by Major Fortes-
cue. His men were engaged in constructing a by-pass along-
the upper weir on the occasion of my inspection His fore-
man informed me that the weir is constructed on the site of an
old weir. There is Ito meation of a weir on the maps of
drai•nage district.
All the tributary drains whiéh are of rather large size are.
choked, some badly, and the tidal sluices 111 bad condition.
One tributary requires particular mention, viz—that from
the river Fane through the townland of Commons, through Mr-
A. Murdock's land, and continued for a short distance along.
what is known as the ' 'Canal." Water from Drorniskin is eon-.
veyed by a drain along the publie road and is disc'harged into
the Fane tributary at a culvert on the public road to the south-
west of Commons three roads. The Fane tributary is badly
choked with weeds, etc, and by six 'badly constructed pass cul-
verts on Mr Murdock's land. Mr M urdock contends that the
wåter from Dromiskin should not be sent along this drain, but
should rather discharge through the (Marsh Farm, via the
• ' 'Canal." This latter ' 'Canal" is not included amongst the
Fane Drainage Works.
A rough estimate of the cost of cleaning, etc, would be about
C650-e700.
13
When the drainage work of the districts in question was
coriginally carried out in 1851, certain owners and proprietors
held the lands charged as shown by the awards. Most of the
lands have sice changed owners, I understand, having been
sold to the tenants under the Land Acts, so that the maps and
awards herewith clo not now show the owners, and the maps
are incomp!ete for assessment purposes.
The re-assessmen€ would be a difficult and troublesome
dertaking. A new survey of the lands involved will be re-
quider, showing area and valuation of each holding. Before
(Iceiding to take over the drainage schemes the Council should
ascertain if the Board of Works is willing to undertake this
survey and assessment. Tile original expenditure in 1851 am-
ounted to CS, 155 15s (Sd, of which C92(j 3s ld was presented by
the Grand Jury of Louth in respect of certain works chargeable
to the County in this District.
As to the liabilities and powers of the Council in ease they
take over the drainage districts, as well as the incidence of tho
necessary expenditure including legal expenditure, stationery,
etc, as well as the actual cost of the works, I would suggest,
that this is a matter for your solicitor, Mr O'Connell,
Your obedient Servant,
THOMAS WALSH.
After discus.sion Che following resolution was passed

ISSCRASCE COMMITTEE FOR THE COUNTY OF

Dear Sir,—
r have been requested by the Insurance Corn—
mission (Ireland), to ascertain from the Louth County Council

TAAFFE'S CASTLE, CARLINGFORD.
The following letter was read and referred to the Finance
Committee : —
20th July, 1914.
The Members of Louth County Council.
Dear Sirs,
May I be allowed, on behalf of the Co. Louth
Archaeological Society, to respectfully suggest to the County
Council the desirability of its acquiring "Taaffe's Castle" in
'Carlingford, which I understand could be obtained at a very
small sum by application to the owner (Lord de V esci OT Mrs
Stewart).
It was offered to the Council in 1901 or 1902, but it
still remains in the owner's hands, and he will probably be as
anxious now as then to get it disposed of.
castle is a very interesting one, in unusually good pre-
•servation, and is a fine historical monument, which would be
unost, fittingly in the ownership of the Public Body that repre-
:sents the County as a whole.
We are confident that the members of the Council will feel
that, this generation owes it to the past and to the future, and
to the national spirit of the country to preserve all public
monuments and prevent the danger of their falling into the
hands of private persons, who might demolish them or let them
fall into decay.
Yours truly,
JOSEPH DOLAN,
One of the Vice-Presidents of the Louth
Archaeological Society.

'BYE-LAWS WITH RESPECT TO THE USE OF INDECENT
EXPRESSIONS AND CONDUCT.
1. No person shall, in any street or public place or on land
•adjoining or near Co sue'.l street, make •use of any abusive,
•profane, blasphemous, indecent or obscene language, be-
haviour or conduct, to the annoyance or disturbance of any
person in such street or place.
2. Any person acting contrary to the foregoing Bye-law
' shall be liable for every such offence to a fine of Forty
Shillings ; provided, nevertheless, that the Justices or Court
•before whom any complaint may be made, or any proceedings
may be taken in respect of any such offence, may, if they think
' fit, adjudge the payment of a fine of any sum less than the full
: amount of the fine imposed by this Bye-law.

TIXSHING OF DROGHEDA COURTHOUSE SEWERS.
The following letter on above matter was read, and the
'Committee recommended that no change be made in the
present system, as payment by meter arrangement was con-
- sidered more satisfactory than by a fixed rate per annum :—
Town Clerk's Office,
Drogheda, 27th July, 1914.
The Secretary,
Coun€y Council of Louth, Dundalk.
Dear Sir,
The County Council pay to the Corporation a fixed charge of
2s (id per quarter for flushing Drogheda Courthouse Sewers,
and by Meter for the water supply in such Courthouse. The
Meter consumption is small, the average for the past two years
being 7,600 gallons, the average sum paid per quarter being
Ils Gd. Through an oversight, Che usual Meter Rent of 2s Gd
per quarter has not been charged.
Alay I suggest that there should be one fixed charge of, say,
per annum, payable half-yearly? This would obviate the
necessity for meter readings and quarterly payments, and
•would be more in accord v;ith our system 'here, as we do not
on Meters except where the consumption is large.
Yours faithfully,
J. B. CONNOLLY.

AUTUMN SHEEP DIPPING
ORDER OF
The following circular letter
Agriculture was read
(IRELAND) TEMPORARY
1914.
from the Department of-
Department of Agriculture {Old Technical Instruction for
Ireland,
(Veterinary Branch),
50 and 51,
No. 294814.
Sir,
With further
Upper Mount Street, Dublin,
28th July, 1914.
to the special efforts for eradicatin'k
43
Sheep Scab in Ireland, I have to transmit, for the information
of your Local Authority, the accompanying supply of copies of-
an Order made by the Department on 27th instant and entitled
the "Autumn Sheep Dipping (Irland) Temporary Order of 1914."
This Order, as will bé observed, is designed to secure that during
the autumn period of the present year, i.e., between the 1st
proximo and 15th November next, sheep throughout Ireland
shall be dipped twice (instead of once as in other years), with an
interval of not less than seven days and not more than fourteen
days between the dippings.
Tt is very advisable that steps should at once bc taken on behalf
of your Local Authority to draw the attention of their Inspectors
to this new Order, and to have a notice as to the scope of the
Order inserted in a news.paper circulating iu their district.
Some copies of a form of notice suitable for this purpose will be
found enclosed.
The police are also being a•pprised regarding the Order, and
supplies of Forms for use thereunder will be sent to the Poliee•
Stations for the convenience of sheep owners.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.
The Clerk
of each Local Authority.
The provisions of the Order were examined and it was set out
therein that all sheep in the Dipping Area during any part of'
the Autumn Dipping period shall be caused, by the owner or
person in charge thereof, to be dipped within. fhe Dipping Area.
on two occasions, separated by an interval of not less than seven
days or more than fourteen days, during that period.
It was decided to issue posters, fo be posted throughout
County, giving the terms of the Order, and also to insert an,
advertisement in the "Dundalk Democrat" and "Droghedu:
44
Argus" to the 11Ke effect, so fliat all sheep owners should oe made
. aware of [he necessity for compliance with its provisions.
the Di ppi ng
It was also unanimously decided that
Superintendents of the Council's tanks should carry out the
regulations regal«ling the Dipping as set out ill the Order.

DEATH OF THE POPE.
The following resolution was
Proposed by Thomas Murphy, E.sq, and
Seconded by Peter B Moore, Esq,
That we, the members of the Louth County Council join in
2
the universal sorrow now prevailing, and not confined to any
,elime or country, in proclaiming the loss sustained by tille world
through the lamented cleatll of his Holiness Pius X. , whose
.airn was to renew all things in Christ, and whose work for man-
kind earned for him the title of "People's Pope," while extend-
ing onr respectful syanpathy to His Eminence Cardinal Logue in
the sad circumstances surrounding the bereavement of the Ca-
t•holic Church.
In putting the resolution to the meeting the Chairman said
that though differing from the majority of the Cijuncil in creed,
..he wished to associate himself most heartily with the expression
Of sorrow that Mr Murphy 'had voiced on 'behalf of the Council.
Sir Vere Foster al.so as-sociated himself with the resolution,
The resolution was passed in silence.

WAR DISTRESS COMMITTEE.
The following letter, together wit'h letters on same subject,
13
dated August 12th and 14th, were read: and were refered to the.
Finance Committee :—
((Copy).
No. 42142/1914.
Miscellaneous.
Local Government Board,
Dublin, 10th August, 1914.
Sir,
I am directed by the Local Government Eoard for
Ireland to inform you that the Government have constituted a
Committee, of which the President of tho English Local Go-
vernment Board is acting as Chairman, to advise on the mea-
sures necessary to deal witih any distress that may arise in
consequence of the war.
It is proposed that there should be a local Committee in
each Borough, or large Urban District, and in each County ex—
elusive of those areas.
The Board do not apprehend that in the Rural Districts of
Ireland there will be any immediate danger of distress arising
from a shortage of the faad supply, as the harvest promises .
well; but the working population in the Urban Districts will
be in the same position as those in the cities and towns of
England in the event of the war continuing for a long period.
The interests of town and country are, however, to a great ex-
tent bound up together, and on these grounds it is desirable .
that in the counties, as well as in tho cities and town.s, there
should be an organisation in existence to meet any emergency
which may arise.
The Board, accordingly, suggest that you should take im-
mediate steps to establish such a Committee, of which you will
bo Chairman. The Committee should comprise representatives .
14
•of local authorities, trades unions and philanthropic organisa-
tions. It is illiportallt that some of the mellibers should be
women.
The Eaard consider it would be desirable, 'especially in the
C rhan Districts, that sellerne:s for employment should be pre-
pared in aclvauee which could be info execution in the
• event of a serious lack of employment arising.
The Loral Government Board will be happy to facilitate tho
local authorities in the matter, and they understand that the
Road Eoard and the Development Commissioners will be pre-
pared to aid suitable schemes within the scope of their powers.
A national relief fund is about to be organised, and it is in-
tended, should distress •arise, that the local distribution of the
•sums thus made available shall be entrusted to the Commit-
tees, over which the Mayors and Chairmen of Councils will pre-
side.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Seeretary.
To the Chairman of each
Countv Council.

PROSECUTION UNDER SALE OF FOOD AND DRUGS ACTS
The following letter from the Council's Solicitor was read and
initialled by the Chairman
Francis Street, Dundalk,
27th August, 1914..
Sale of Food and Drugs Acts.
Dear Sir,
T beg to report that T attended Collon Petty Sessions
to-day on behalf of the County Council on hearing of summons
against Edward Garg-an, Collon, that defendant "did sell to the
prejudice of the purchaser an article of food, €0 wit, butter,
which was not Of the nature, substance and quality of the article.
demanded."
The Department's Inspector proved the ease, and Certificato of
the Council's Analyst showed the sample of butter contained 22.2
per cent of water, the maximum being 16 per cent. Defendant.
appeared and was represented by Mr P Tallan, Solicitor,.
Drogheda.
The Magistrates convicted defendant xnd fined him IOS and IOS.
costs.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL

APPLICATION FOR EXEMPTION PROM RATES.
The following letter from Rev John Byrne, P.P., Dunleer, was
road, and exemption was recommended
Dunleer,
August 25th, 1914.
tlemen,—
As one of the Trustees for Dunleer Total Abstinence
Hall, I wish to claim exemption from rates for same.
25
This Hall was built about nine years ago by tho subscriptions
«of some people in favour of the Temperance Movement,
the ptnnies of the mem beys themselves, but principally by tho
proceeds of a Bazaar on a small scale, organised by myself, and is
kept going since by the little contributions of the Society.
It is held for the eommunity by three Trustees—Archdeacon
. Segrave, myself, and Rev A. Donnell, C.C., and, needless to say,
was erected for the sole benefit and protection of the working
men and boys Of Dunleer who would become Total Abstainers.
We are making every effort to keep the Society alive at all, and
its funds at the present time are so limited as to be quite inade-
-quato for such a tax as rates.
Trusting, for these reasons, that my claim for exemption will
receive your favourable consideration.
I am, Gentlemen,
Faithfully yours,
JOHN BYRNE, P.P.
The Members of'
Louth County Council.

Sir,—
I am (lil ected by the Local Government Board for Ireland'
to state that inquiries continue to be addressed €0 them by Loeul
Authorifi€s asking what arrangements they may adopt in the ea.s€»
Of any of their employees who join his Majesty's
The Board are aware that some Local Authorities have already
decided to give half-pay towards the support of dependants of
their employ€es who have been summoned for Naval or Military
Service.
The Board wish it €0 be understood that not alone in the case.
Of employees who have previously joinod His Majesty's Forces,
but also in the case Of those who now wish to do so, they will
raiS@ no objection to leave of absence boing given to such em-
ployees and to the Local Authorities making a reasonable pay-
ment in respect of wages or salaries to persons authorised by suclv
Further, the Board will assent
employees receive money.
where nteessary to l.ccal Authorities employing and paying tem-
porary substitutes for officers who are summom«l or volunteer for
Naval and for Military Service.
Appended to this letter will be found the arrangements mado.
in the case of Civil Servants on Naval or Military Service.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARI AS,

To the Secretary of each Co. Council.
TREASURY REGULATIONS FOR CIVIL SERVANTS.
The Treasury Regula tions and Instructions of the 11th and
20th August, 1914, dealing with thc ease of Civil Servants on Navar
or Military Sa•vice, provide for the payment to them or to their
reprcsentativcs during their absence from Civil duty of their full
civil pay, less a deduction on account of Navy or Army pay and
allowances. For all ranks below Commissioned Officer this dew
duetion will be at the rate of seven shillings a week, together
with the actual amount of any Army separation allowance paid
to the family of a married man. As 1 egards a Commissioned
Officer the deduction will be equal to his active service pay
(allowances being neglected). The civil posts of these officers and
men will not be permanently filled during their absence on Naval
or Military Service, and that service will count for Civil Pension
and for increments of Civil Salary.
TRADE BOARDS ACT, 1909.
CONTRACTS OF LOCAL ACTHORITIES.
Circular letter from the Government Boar-a, dated 8th
September, stating that the Board of Trade have made orders
making obligatory in cases in which they are applicable, on all
persons employing labour and on all persons employed, the
minimum rates of wages as varied by the Chain Trade Board and
the minimum rates of wages fixed by the Tailoring Trade Board
(Ireland). The effect of the obligatory orders is to make it illegal
for wages to be paid at less than the minimum rates fixed, except
in the ease of timo workers who huve obtained spt•cinl permits from
the Trade Board exeqnpting their employment. It will not now be
necessary for an employer to give notice to the Trade Board that he
is willing that the minimum rate should be obligatory on him in
order to be eligible to roceive from a Government Department
or Authority a contract involving employment to which
the minimum rates are applicable.

MONTHLY REPORTS OF VETERINARY INSPECTORS
FOR AUGUST, 1914.
The following reports were read and initialled by the Chair-
man
Sir,—
CLINTON'S REPORT.
I beg to report that during the month of August
two outbreaks of Sheep Seab occurred at Dundalk Port, one in
:sheep the property of Mr T Malone, Drumsharnbo, and the
•other in sheep the property of Mr Thomas Clos.sy, of Navan.
38
The necessary steps have 'been taken in both cases and! the av
fected sheep slaughtered.
Two cases also occurred under the Tuberculosis Order in
cows the property of J Duddy, Limavady. They were sent
forward for shipment at Dundalk, and were detained under the.
provisions of the Order. Both cows were slaughtered by order
of the owner, and your Council is not liable for any compensa-
tion in the matter.
Your Obedient Servant,
J. T. CLINTON.
MR. DONNELLY'S REPORT.
Drogheda, Sept. 6th, 1914.
Veterinary Report for August, 1914 (Drogheda Union).
Gentlemen,
During the month of August I inspected the fairs.
and markets in this district.
Two suspected cases were notified to me under the Tuberculo-
sis Order, and I dealt with same, and sent reports to your Se-
cretary.
The district at present is free from any outbreak of conta-
gious disease in animals. One or two suspected cases of conta-
gious diseases reported to me, proved on inspection not to bee
notifiable diseases.
Yours respectfully,
ANDREW A. DONNELLY.
To the Louth Co. Council.
(Executive Committee).

Ardee, 1st Sept., 1914.
The Secretary,
IA)uth County Council, Dundalk.
Sir,—
I beg to report that I visited the usual fairs, markets,
etc, held in the Ardee No. I District for the month of Au-
gust.
Though the sheep detained on the farm of R A 13 Henry,
at Rathnestin, Tallanstown, are free from disease this last
month, I cannot issue a withdrawal notice as the premises havo•
not yet been disinfected. I am reporting this to the Deparv
ment of Agriculture who deal with such cases.
In reply to your communication of the '26th ult, I went to.
the Ardee Fair Green and examined sheep, which were healthy.
In conjunction with Sergeant Kenny, T made inquiries and'
learned that these sheep had been on Mr Rooney's land at
Derrycamma for some time, and as the man in charge
a Louth declaration of dipping I let them go.
I am, Sir,
Faithfully yours,
W. E. MAXWELL.

DREDGING AT ANNAGASSAX.
The following letter from the Department of Agriculture was
read
Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction,
(Fisheries Branch),
3, Kildare Place, Dublin,
18th September, 1914.
,929-'14-F.B.
Sir,
In reference to the harbour works at Annagnssan,
which are now being carried out by the Department under
.agreement with the Louth County Council, J have to state, for
i he information of the Council, that the Department have de-
Tided to suspend dredging operations for the present season,
:since the days are becoming too short to permit of the economic
,use of the dredging plant on tidal work.
I have further to state that the Department are of opinion
that it would be unwise, at the present time, to make the pro-
posed cut from the basin at the new quay to the upper part of
•the river, for the following reasons :
The bank, which it was proposed to cut through, and which
was assumed to be composed of the same stiff material as the
rest of the ground excavated, has proved to be loose shingle.
The effect of diverting the current of the river through a out
made in this material might not improbably be to fill up the
basin with shingle washed from the sides and bottom of the
45
eut which would have a considerable fall from its upper end to»
the basin. Moreover, the course of a cut made through such'
material would be liable to change, and the stability cf the.
quay might be thus endangered.
After observation •of the effect of winter floods and gales on
the part of the bank adjoining the basin, the Department's
gineer will .be in a better position to advise on the question or
making the ent.
I am, Sir,
The Secretary,
Your Obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL, Secretary.
Louth County Council.
Courthouse, Dundalk.
A discussion arose on this matter, Mr Hamill suggesting thac
the letter be referred to the Annagassan Harbour Commission—
ers.
Mr Murphy stated he wished to propose a resolution on, the
subject requesting the Department to proceed with the work
at once, and not postpone it till next year.
The Chairman asked the County Surveyor his opinion on the
matter, and he (Mr Walsh) stated that it would be desirable to
let the dredging remain till next Spring, and see the effect of
the winter floods, and suggested the possibility of obtaining a
grant from the Development Commissioners.
Mr Murphy withdrew his motion, and the following resolution
was unanimously passed :—
Proposed by Bernard Hamill, Esq, J P;
Seconded by Wm T Skeffington, E.sq, J P, and
Resolved—That the letter of the Department of Agriculture
•ith reference to the dredging at Annagassan, 'be referred to
•thc Harbour Commissioners of Annagassall for their considera-
•tien.
TUBERCULOSIS COMMITTEE OF MANAGEMENT AND
DROGHEDA TUBERCU%OSIS DISPENSARY.
A deputation from the Tuberculosis Committee of Manago-
rnent consisting of Dr M 'Quillan, T M O, and Mr V Warren,
•Clerk, was heard 'by the Council in reference to the Drogheda
Tuberculosis Dispensary.
Dr M 'Quillan stated he wished to bring under the notice
of the Council the great difficulty that was experienced in pro-
perly attending to tuberculosis patients in Drogheda, owing
to the want of a Dispensary where they could be properly
treated; that it was difficult to get the Drogheda, Sub-Commit-
tee to meet to make arrangements about the provision of a dis-
pensary, and that the failure to provide same was 'holding up
the contributions from the Insurance Commissioners.
A protracted discussion ensued on this matter, and other
matters pertaining to the Co. Louth scheme for the treatment,
of Tuberculosis, in which Messrs Skeffington, Hamill, M 'Quil-
Ian and Murphy took part, and ultimately tile following reso-
lution was passed •
Proposed Bernard Hamill, Esq, J P;
Seconded bv T V M'Qui11an, Esq, and
Resolved—That we -hereby request the Drogheda Members of
the Tuberculosis Committee of Management to take the neces-
sary steps provide a Dispensary in Drogheda for the accom-
modation of tuberculosis patients—the cost of same not to ex
cgoo.

PASSING, OF THE HOME RULE BILL.
The following resolution was passed unanimously
Preposed Wm A Doran, Esq, J P;
Seconded by Thomas M 'Court, Esq, and
Resolved—That we desire to express our unbounded satisfac-
tion at the passing of the Home Rule Bill, and to express a wish
that Irishmen will now sink every difference of creed and elars,
and work loyally together for the uplifting and advancement
of cur be!cved country.
T.iat we congratulate Mr John E Redmond and the Irish
Party on the triumphant issue of their protracted labours and
-sacrifices, and assure them of our sincere appreciation of their
great work in recovering our Native Parliament.

puf up by them would be;—

As regerds the Rural Disfriefs Che Board submit a financial
scheme which I lay before you. From this if will be seen that
the total cost to the County spread over seven years from 1914
to 1921 is 88, 491, that is of course for the work of improve-
Tn ofhtr Countics, e.g., Dublin, Meath, Armagh, and Down. tho

however, did not get loans free of interest from the Board for

by way of grant, the cost to the Rural Districts would work out:

was suggested that the Co. Surveyor, accompanied by the

COMPENSATION FOR DESTRUCTION OF FRUIT TREES.
The following circular letter from the Department of Agricul-
•ture was read, and referred to the County Council
Department of Agriculture and Technical Instruction for
Ireland,
Upper Merrion Street, Dublin,
10th August, 1914.
Sir,—
I have to transmit herewith, for the information of the
•Co. (huncil of Louth, a copy of an Order made by the Depart-
ment under the Destructive Insects and Pests Acts, 1877 and 1907,
for the purpose of preventing the spreading in Ireland of American
Gooseberry Mildew and Black Currant Mite. The injurious re-
sults of these diseases and their highly infectious nature are ex-
plained in the Department's leaflets Nos. 47 and 70, eopies of
Nhich are enclosed.
It will be observed that, under the Order, fruit growers may be
required to destroy gooseberry bushes affected with American
'Gooseberry Mildew, as well as Black Currant bushes affected
Black Currant Mite. When the disea.se known as American
'Gooseberry Mildew was first dealt. with by the Department, the
56
spraying and pruning of diseos:xl bushes was in some instances;
permitted as an experiment to ascertain whether sueh treatment
would succeed in controlling the disease.
Tlaving rcgnrcl to the.
results of the experiments so far carried out, and to the infor-
mation at the disposal of their Plant Disease Division, the De-
partment are of opinion that the only effective method of pre-
venting the spread of the disease in Ireland is fo have the bushes
destroyed. Similarly, the destruction of infected bushes has also.
been found to be the only satisfactory means of duding with
Black Currant Mite. Consequently, the usual procedure is to,
serve a notice on the occupier of €110 premises on which diseased
bushes are found, requiring him to have the bushes and
bilined. As the spores of American Gooseberry Mildew are-
borne not only on. the leaves and stems of gooseberry bushes, but
also on the fruit, it is necessary to prohibit the Inarketing of any
berries from diseas:d bushes.
Applications for compensation have recently been received
from fruit growers whose bushes or fruit hove had to be de-.
stroyed in pursuance of noticas under the Order. A memorandum
referring fo the Sections in the Aet dealing with payment Of
compensation is enclosed, from which if will be observed that the
Acts provide for tho payment of compensation of the local
rate. The Department are advised that these payments are to be,
borne on the general county rates and not on the special rate
struck för the purposes of the Agriculture and Technical ln-
struction Act of 1899. It will be noted that while the order•
directing payment-of compensation is to be made by the Depart-
menf, Seetion 1 (1) of the Destructive Insects and Pests Act of 1907
declares in effect that such Order shall not be made without tho•
consent of the County Council, who are now Che local authority
for the purposes of the Acl.
Should the County Council be
willing that an Order Of the nature referred to should be made
by the Department, it is suggested that a resolution to that effect
should be adopted by the County Cou•ncil, and that a. sealed copy
should be forwarded to the Department.
Should there be any additional information which the Depart-
ment may be able to afford and which would assiSt the Council'
57
in coming to a decision on this matter, the Department will bc.
glad to furnish it if so requested.
I am, Sir,
Your Obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL, Secretary.

COMMITTEE FOR REVISION OF STANDING ORDERS.
Minutes of Proccedings of above Committee af their Meeting
held in the Council Chamber, Courthouse, Dundalk, at one o'clock
p m on Monday, 5th October, 1914.
Present :—
In the Chair—Wm A Doran, Esq, J P.
Also present—T Murphy and Bernard Hamill, J P, Esquires.
The Committee had before them the printed Standing Orders of
the Council and discussed various amendments. It was found
that alterations had been made heretofore in the printed Orders
by rcsolutions of the County Council. These alterations were
, as follows :—
Standing Order No. IO to read—"The Quarterly Meetings of tho
•Council shall be held on Thursday next succeeding 17th January,
17th April, 22nd July, and 10th November. The Adjourned
Quarterly Meetings of the Council shall be held on the last
Thursday in May, November, and February, and the third
'Thursday in August."
Standing Order No. 16 to hour of meeting of tho
•Council shmll be I o'clock, excepting the Annual Meeting, which
•hall be held at 11 0'clock."
Standing Order No. 17 to all Meetings of tho
•Council, with the exception of the Annual Meeting (which shall
59
held in Drogheda), shall be held in the Courthouse, Dundalk,
unless it be otherwise provided for by a resolution of the
•vCouneil."
Standing Order No. 61 to Committee of the whole
Council is composed of all the Members of the Council, five con-
• stituting a quorum (save and except u Finance Committee, three
Members of which shall form a quorum), and it transacts such
business as is from time to time committed to it by order of the
' Council."
The Committee unanimously decided fliat. Standing Older No. 5
. should be altered to read as follows a Councillor is absent
from Meetings of the Council for more than six months, except
in the case of illncsss, or for some reasons approved of by the
• Council, his office shall, on the expiration of these months, be-
come vacant."
This alters the term of "Twelve Months," as it stood in the
printed Orders, to "Six Months."
The Cotnmittee also decided to delete Standing Older 18, which
Chair is to be taken at a Meeting of the Council with-
in a quarter of an hour afto• the time appointed for such Meeting
by the Chairman, or, in his absence, by the or
otherwise by any Member called thereto by motion."
• as it is covered by Standing Order No. 19, which at
the expiration Of thirty minutes after the hour at which any
Meeting of the Council be appointed to be held, a quorum shall
not be present, no Meeting shall take place."
Tho (hmmittce ordered that the Standing Orders as now re-
vised be recommended for adoption by the Co. Council.

The Road Board,

Dear Sir,
Dublin-Belfast Road.
I beg to refer to your letter of the 28th ultimo: and to say
that the Road Board regret, having regard to the funds avail-
able, that they are not prepared to make the grants applied for
by your Council, especially when, on your own statement„
there are other roads in the county calling for improvement, in
respect of which the Board assume your Council will apply for
advances.
If, however, your Council decide to borrow the balance of the
cost of the works and the money required for the plant propo—
sed to be purchased, from outside the Board would
prepared, in lieu of tho loans indicated in my letter of the 14th
ultimo, to contribute, in addition to the grants indicated in
that letter, such sums as shall be equal to the interest on such
loans borrowed in the ordinary way and repayable by five equal
annual instalments.
The Board realise that even allowing for these additional
grants the proportion of Che eosi which the Council would have
to bear will represent more than per celit of the cost of the
works, and if your Council decide not to proceed with the pro-
posal with the assistance of the advances indicated by the
Board, it will be open to them to reduce the estimated cost of
the works to be undertaken.
On the other hand if your Council decide to proceed with the
works with the assistance indicated in my letter of the 14th
68
Riltimo, and will return t'he draft statements accompanying Che
same, the matter will be in order; or if they decide to carry
out the works with the assistance indicated above, revised draft,
Statements of the proposed grants will be forwarded as soon as
you let me know the terms on which your Council propose to

painting carried out :—

raise the loans required.
Yours faithfully,
The County Snrveyor,

•elusion could be arrived at was by a personal interview with tho
Road Board in London.
Mr Murphy was rather opposed €0 this view, Tjut ultimately
the following resolution was passed unanimously
by B Hamill, Esq, J P;
by M Meade, Esq,
That a deputation from the County Council consisting of the
'Chairman of the Council, the County Surveyor, and the Secre-
tary proceed to London and intewiew the Road Board with a
view to obtaining better terms from them.
DROGHEDA COU RT HOUSE.
REPORT FROM COUNTY SURVEYOR.
The following report was read, and the matter was left in tho
Dundalk, 10-10-1914.
To the Chairman and Gentlemen Finance Committee of Louth
County Council Meeting, 12-10-'14.
Drogheda Courthouse.
Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
As instructed by you at your last Meeting,
inspected Drogheda Courthouse re painting of same.
The windows and doors in rear portion of Courthouse require
painting; also belfry would be in the better of painting.
Windows to courts require some new cord, and pulleys freed„
As regards painting walls of courts, I do not think this is,
advisable. The plaster is coming off in sheets, or inclined to,
come off, owing I believe to settlements taking place in walls.
The exterior of the Courthouse to rear was originally plas—
tered or dashed with poor mortar which is falling off gradually.
Some cracks are apparent in the walls ,and it may be advisable,
not to do anything to this portion till winter frosts have helped.
to remove Old plastering, and during next summer to eem•ent.
stucco the walls afterm aking good the cracks.
Your Obedient Servant,
THOMAS WAISTE

Minutes of Proceedings of the above C%rnmittee held in the
Council Chamber, Court House, Dundalk, on Thursday, 15th
day of ()ctdber, 1914, at one o'clock, p m.
Present

The following Report from the County Surveyor, which had
-already been before the Committee, was read :—
Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
In accordance with your directions of 9th Mareh last a plan
of the Old Jail premises adjacent to the Courthouse has been
prepared for your consideration.
I had not' the advantage -of boing present at your discussion
on 9th March, and am not quite certain of what is intended ex-
actly by the ' 'additional -accommodation to be provided for"
whether the Committee proposed that the County Council
should continue to use some existing offices in the Courthouse,
and also the Grand Jury Room as a Council Chamber ; or whe-
71
•ther they should erect a complete new suite of offces, with
•Council Chamber, on the 'Old Jail premises.
Assuming *the latter proposition to be correct, the accommo-
qlation required would be:—
(a) Council Chamber, 40 ft x 25 ft, say.
(b) 1 Committee Room, about 16 ft square.
(c) 1 Spare ditto, IG feet, square, to serve as office in busy
times for Rate Clerks, etc.
(d) 1 Single Office about IG feet square—Accountant.
(e) 3 Double Offices about 16 feet square for •Secretary and
Staff and County Surveyor and Staff, and County Committee
'Of Agriculture.
(f) I Single Office somewhat larger, for Secretary Insurance
•Committee.
(g) The necessary Lavatories attached to County Council
Chamber and 'Offices and Cloak Room.
(h) Stationery Store Room for Secretary, 80 to 100 sq. feet
area, say.
(i) Strong Room for Secretary, 80 sq. feet area, say.
(j) Store at baek for coal and sundries. With regard to this
latter, and having also regard to the fact that the Council may
purchase road-rollers, it may not be amiss to consider the ad-
visability and possibility of acquiring an entrance through the
(irban Council premises adjoining.
No mention has been made above of a Store Room and
Strong Room for the Clerk of the Crown and Peace. The bed-
room and kitchen occupied at present by the Courtkeeper could,
without difficulty, so far as I can see, be converted into a Store
Room and Strong Room respectively, and would be convenient
to Mr Sheridan's office, provided, of course, that accommoration
72
could be provided for the Courtkeeper in the part of the Court—
house vacated by the Council.
The question also presents itself as to whether a resident
Caretaker should be appointed in charge of the offices. If so,
a residence, of course, would have to be provided for him.
It should be remembered when considering the proposed site•
that the drain from the Courthouse to the Street sewer•
runs through the centre of the garden in front, if I am rightly
informed. Tn any plan for a building on the plot provision
would have to be made for changing the course of the drain.
Should the (%mmittee decide to advertise for competitive,
plans and specifications, an order should be made to procure
two dozen (say) copies Of site plan to be Supplied Vo architects.
1b would be advisable also, if possible, to have photographs of
the adjacent buildings—east side of C%urthouse and the Ex—
change—to accompany plan of site. The attention of architects.
competing should be called to the question of providing for re—
fleeted light for east side of Courthouse (if necessary).
The terms for competition would 'be a matter to be settled'
by tho Committee after discussion.
It would be desirable also if the Committee would define on_
the plan submitted the limits of the site to be built on.
I am, Mr Chairman and Gentlemen,
Your obedient Servant,
(Signed) THOMAS WALSH.
The ground plan of site, as prepared by the County Surveyor,.
was also before the Committee, and a discussion en•
sued as to the various accommodation to be provided in the,
offices.
Mr Bernard Hamill then proposed, and
Mr Thomas Murphy seconded, and
It was Unanimously eårried :
That we recommend Chat the County Council proceed witlv
the erection of new Offices at a cost not to exceed C6,000, ow
73
the site on the east of the County Court House; that prizes be-
offered for plans for same, for best plan, and C'25
for second best plan; and that Professor W. A. Scott, of the,
National University, be appointed Assessor, and that they
County Surveyor confer with him on the various details as too
accommodation, etc.

DRAFT SCHEME FOR THE TREATMENT OF ADVANCED
CASES OF TUBEIOCULOSIS APPROVED r.Y THE
AUTHORITIES OF THE DUNDALK UNION.
1. The County Council shall be permitted to erect in the
Grounds of the Dundalk Union two chalets, one for male and
one for female patients, €0 accommodate in all 12 beds. A kit-
chen to be erected in conj unction with one •or other of the chal-
ets. Cost of erection and equipment to be defrayed by tixo
82
County Council out of the Treasury Grant available for the..
purpose.
2. A nominal rent to 'be paid for the ground provided.
3. Chalets and equipment to be the property of the County
Council, and shall controlled by the County Council, or such
Representative Body as they may appoint.
4. Council shall be permitted to connect sewerage with main
sewer.
5. That the County Council shall guarantee an annual con-
tribution of C21(j towards the estimated establishment charges
(as set out below), and 7s per week per patient under treat-
ment for maintenance.

REPORT FROM ROAD BOARD DEPUTATION.
The following report from [Ime deputation sent by the Finance
'Committee to interview the Road Board in London on the subject
Of the Grant offet«l by the Road Board for the strengthening and
sulfacing Of the Dublin-Belfast Road, was read
ROAD BOARD GRANT.
REPORT OF DEPUTATION TO THE ROAD BOARD.
The Deputation appointed by the Finance Committee at their
Meeting held on Monday, the 12th October, 1914, to interview the
mad Board on the question Of the Grant for the Dublin-Belfast
road, proceeded to London on Monday evening. The Deputation
consisted of the Chairman of the County Council, tlle (hunty
Surveyor, and the Sceretary. The interview took place by ap-
pointrnent at the Road Board Offices, London, and was introduced
by Mr Nolan, M P for South Louth.
Tho Engineer to the Road Board, Mr Maybury and later Sir
George Gibb, the Chairman of the Road Board, discussed tlle
matter of the Grant with the Deputafion, and it was pointed out
that the amount offered by the Road Board did not lepresent the
pound for pound which harl been suggested as the basis of the
•Grant when the Road Board met the COuneil Representatives in
-Dundalk in November, 1913. Mr Maybury stated the Boa ld
would grant the. County of Louth the half of the amount ex-
pended on the work, but at present the Board were only able to
•offer the sum of C5,450. If. was then arranged that if work to
tho extent of EIO,900 would b? undertaken, the Road Board would
give the half of this amount, a granf of 5,450.
Your Daputation directed attention to the offer of a loan for the
purchase of the neecssary road making machinery, and the En-
.gineer stated the Board would be prepared to pay interest on this
loan of C4,C95 for five years at 3 per cent, the amount fo be bor-
rowed by the Council from outside sources.
A further request by the Deputation for the interest on the
amount of Z5,450 to be put by the C0uneil for their share Of
the work was assented to by the Engineer, and after discussion
Of the figures he agreed to give a grant of the amount of interest
85
.:at 3 per cent on necessary amount to be borrowed by
-the Council and repaid in 5 years.
The summing up Of the result of the interview is tha.t the
Board offer a grant of on a total expenditure of 810,900,
a grant of C368 fo pay interest on the machinery loan, and a
further grant of C436 fo pay interest on the loan to be raised by
the Council for the work.
We consider the offer highly satisfactory, and that it should be
accepted.
(Signed),
WILLIAM A. DORAN,
THOMAS WALSH,
CHARLES M'ALESTER.
23rd October, 1914.
A discussion took place on the Report, and the Committee ex-
pressed satisfaction that the Deputation had succeeded in arrang-
ing such favourable terms for 'the County, and on the motion of
ALr C A Duffy, seconded by Mr T Murphy, the Committee
unanimously dc eided to recommend the County Council to accept
the terms.
The question of the money to be borrowed by the County
Council for the purchase of road making machinery and for the
money to be put up by the County Council for their share of the

INSURANCE OF CLERKS IN COUNTY OFFICE.
A letter was read from Mr Doyle, Inspector under the National
Health Insurance Aet relative to the exemption from Insurance
under the A ef of the Clerks in the County Council Office.
letter stated that under First Schedule, Part 11. , par. b of the
Act, the Insurance Commissioners would grant exemption to the
employees of a local or public authority where they were satisfied
that the terms of employment were such as to secure provision in
respect of sickness and disablement on the whole not less favour-
able than the corresponding benefits eonferred by Part I. Of the
Act.
On the motion of Mr Thomas Murphy,
Seconded by Mr Thomas O'Rourke,
It was unanimously recommended that the County Council
should give the guarantee necessary to secure exemption from.
Insurance of the two Assistants in the Secretary's Office.

Mr M'Keever's Engineer having failed to keep his appointment'.
A letter from Mr M 'Keever addressed to the Clutirman was also
read, requesting permission to appear before [he Committee,
which request was acceded to.
Mr M'Keever explained to the Committee that the cause of thc»
delay was Lowing to the fact that appointment made between
his Engineer, Mr Storey, and Mr Dromgoole, Assistant Surveyor,
had not taken place. Mr Dromgoole stated that he had gone•
specially to Ardee, but thiLt Mr Storey did not keep Che ap-
pointment. This was substantiated by the County Surveyor,
who stated that neither Mr M 'Keveer nor his Engineer had ear-
ried out the arrangements made for the cheeking of fhcy map. It:
wws eventually arranged that Mr M "Keever's Engineer would
attend at Ardee on Thursday, 29th insf, to meet the County Sur-
veyor and check the map. On the application of Ur M 'Keever,
the Committee recommended that any additional poles that niight
require to be erected under the Electric Lighting Scheme might
be done without the preparation of a separate agreement, but
that the County Solicitor should instert a Clause in the present
Agreement that any additional polos required could bo erected,
provided Mr M'Keever and his Conipany furnished a map showing
the position in which the additional poles would be erected, which
map would be furnished to the County Surveyor and his consent
obtained to the erection of the poles.

BOAT SLIP AT GILES' QUAY.
Tho Secretary brought under the notice of the Committee that
the Boat House and Slip at Giles' Quay were to be sold by public
auction on this day, Monday, the 26th October, and that some
members of the County Council considered that as the Slip was,
us€d in connection with Giles' Quay Harbour, and had been used
for a number of years for the landing of fish, that some, action
should be taken to notify the AuctionetT that, the Couneii (Iainjed
The Seercfary further informed
the Slip as County
the Committee thaf Mr O'Connell, fhe Council's Solicitor, was in
communication with the Board of Works to obtain title to the
Slip which had been claimed by MI' Mackay, who had obtained
possassion of the Boat House.
Mr Duffy considered that the right of the COuneiI to the Slip
should have been contested when this claim of Mr Mackay's to
the Slip had been set up by prosecuting those fishermen who had
landed fish at the Slip without paying the poundage fees, which.
had been the custom for thirty-five years.
The following resolution was
Proposed by Mr James Wynne,
Seconded by Mr Thomas Murphy:
"That we advise Mr O'Connell €0 appear at the sale of the.
Giles' Quay Boat House and Slip, and inform the Auctioneer that
the Louth County (fijuncil claim the Slip as County property, and
object to the sale of same."
For this resolution there voted —Messrs W A Doran, James .
Wynne, J F Murphy, T Murphy, J Halpenny, and T O'Rourke—6.
Against—Mr C A Duffy—I.
The resolution was declarcd carried, and WOI@I was sent to Mr
O'Connell accordingly.
Subsequently Mr O'Connell appeared before the Committee.
and informed them that he had warned the Auctioneer not to.
92
proceed with the sale, but notwithstanding his protest the sale
proceeded, and the Boat House and Slip were sold for 260.
After discussion, when Mr Duffy pointed out that something
should have been done to secure the Slip to the County Council,
having been under their control and that of the Grand Jury for
thirty-seven years, Mr CVCOnneII stated that he was in communi-
cation with the Board of Works and that they stated that they
could not clear up the title. Mr O'Connell a l,so read eounsel's
opinion which he had obtained, and in which hej held that
the Council had a good ease for claiming the Slip.
The Committee unanimously decided [hat Mr O'Connell should
serve notice on the purchaser that the (Avunty Council are claim-
ing the Slip and the right €0 collect fees, aud to take whateveru
action was necessary for securing the Council's property; and
report the matter fully to the next meeting of the County
Council.

SHEEP DIPPING NOTICES.
Mr Patrick M •Gee referred to prosecutions under the Sheep
Dipping Order, and stated that he tj.lought it was unfair that
persons wt10 had dipped their sheep at the Council's tanks
•should be prosecuted for not serving the notice of having dip-
ped on the police. He thought it should be the duty of the
Dipping Superintendent to serve the necessary nctices.
The Secretary submitted copy of the Order. but after dis-
cussion, the following resolution was
Proposed by Patrick M 'Gee, Esq, J P ;
Seconded by Thomas Murphy, Esq, and
Resolved—Tha€ it b? the duty of the Dipping Superinten-
dents nt the Council's tanks to notify the police when a sheep
owner dips his sheep at the Council's tanks.

CHAIRMAN'S RESIGNATION NOT ACCEPTED.
The Chairman, in an eloquent, speech, in which he outlined
-the reasons for his action, te!ndered his resignation as Chair-
man of the County Council. He informed the Council that,
the dictates of his conscience, and the call of his Leader, Mr
-John E Redmond, to stand by the British Empire, now that,
she had granted Home Rule to Ireland, had compelled him as a
matter of duty to offer his services as a private soldier €0 fight
against the grinding militarism which aimed at the domination
of all the nationalities of Eum•ope.
Mr M 'Carfhy moved that) the Chairman's resignation be not
•accepted, and stated fiat, he thought that the Chairman had
•acted logically according to his views, and that what was done
for the humblest member of the community on active service
should bo done for their Chairman, namely, to keep his posi-
fion open.
Mr Skeffington seconded the resolution, and stated that
8
they had all listened with pleasure to the Chairman's spirited'
address.
A number of the Councillors supported Mr M 'Cårthy's mo—
tion, and eventually
The Chairman withdrew his resignation, and thanked the•
Council for their kindly feelings towards him in the circum-
stances.

the Adjourned Quarterly Meeting on 26th November:—
Francis Street, Dundalk,
26th August, 1914.
Re Wottonstown and Fane Drainage Districts.
Dear Sir,—
Adverting to previous correspondence, I am instructed
by the Louth County Council to inquire from your Board to what
extent they may rely on your Board dealing with the matters set
out héreunder before finally coming to a decision as to taking
over the business of åbove-mentioned districts
The re-assessment of the respective districts in view of the-
change of ownership under the Land Purchase Acts.
15
The adjusting of contentious questions that have arisen byr
reason of the failure Of the Trustees to carry out their trust.
Such as, the erection of two weirs by Major Fortescue, the ob-
struction by Murdock at Commons and Craig at Walterstown,.
and similar questions of right claimed by tenant purchasers to
have been acquired by prescription.
Generally, any information by your Board will bé welcomed on
the subject of how far the Council will be protected in the event;
of deciding to take over the works, that they may be in posi-
tion to measure the extent of their prospective liabilities.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL
The Secretary,
Office of Vuhlic Works, Dublin.
Office of Public Works, Dublin,
9th September, 1914.
12479/14.
Sirs—
I am directed by the Commissioners of Public Works to
refer to your letter of 26th ultimo, inquiring what would be tho,
position of the County Council if they took over the business of
the Wottonstown and Fane Drainage Districts under the pro-
visions of Section 20 of the Local Government (Ireland) Acts
1898, and I am to forward herewith copies Of the Awards made
by the Board on completion of the Works of the District which
will afford you information as to the cost and nature of the
works, the extent of the District, and the lands benefitted by tho,
Drainage. Speaking generally, the business Of the Trustees
consists in seeing that the Drainage works are properly main-
tained, and in levying annually on the lands improved by the
operation of the works a sum sufficient for the maintenance
works and other expenses incident thereto. The duties of the-
Trustees are more particularly set forth in Section CXXT. of the
Act, 5 and 6 Vic., Cap. 89. Copy of Section enclosed. Tho
16
County Council of Kildare have taken over the business of eight
Drainage Districts under the provisions of Section 20 of the Local
Government (Ireland) Act, 1898, and tne Secretary or the County
Surveyor would probably on inquiry furnish you with details of
the working of the business of these Districts by the County
Council.
As regards the two points specially mentioned in your letter of
the 26th ultimo, namely, the assessment of new proprietors and
the settlement of outstanding contentious questions, there is no
difficulty in regard to the former. All that is necessary for the
person acting as Superintendent or Secretary to do is to forward
a map showing the boundaries of the new proprietors' holdings,
with Schedule of the names of the new proprietors to the
Board, when a. Deed apportioning the liability for the charge for
maintenance over the lands of the new proprietors will be
execu ted.
As regards the question of the removal of obstructions to the
flow of the water, the Board are aware of one recent ease in
which the Trustees after legal action succeeded in compelling the
removal of weirs which had been illegally placed in the channels
causing an obstruction to the flow of the water in the District.
If the County Council take over the duties of the Trustees in the
Wottonsfovvn and Fane Districts, and if in either of these Dis-
tricts weirs have been illegally placed obstructing the flow of the
wafer, if. will be the duty of the County Coeneil to take what-
ever steps they may be advised €0 be necessary for the removal
of theso obstructions and to charge the cost of any such pro-
ceedings against the amount collected as maintenance rate
from the proprietoxs of lands within the District.
The Board have no information in respect to the v obstructions
mentioned by you, and they are, therefore, unable to offer any
advice in the matter.
1 am €0 add that any further information you may require the
Board will be happy to give you.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
H. WILLIAMS,
Secretary.

REPORT ON TAAFFE'S CASTLE, CARLINGFORD.
We, the members of the Sub-Committee appointed by the co.
Council to report the conditions on which Taaffeps Castle, Car-
lingford, could acquired by the County Cbuneil, bag fo sulnni€
to the Council that while it would be Inost desirable for the
COuneiI to undertake the contract of €110 entire building, we are
Of opinion an earnest effort ought to be made to obtain the por-
tiou of this Castle at present in the possession of the V esci
family. This consists of the. tower of the castle, and it is of
special historic interest.
We are strongly of opinion that this amount of purchase to Ije
paid to the local owners of the other portion of the eastle, even
in case were willing to sell, would prevent any further' ae-
fion on the part of the Council.
As the castle has both archaeological and historic interests for
the people of Louth, and as De Vesei made an offer to flie
County Council a few years ago, we hope the Council will re-open
m•gotiation.s with liis representative,s, with a view to acquire the
guardianship of a most intercsting relic of the past.
QUIN, V.P.,
J. P. KEARNEY,
W. WOODS.

GYIÆS' QUAY BOAT SLIP.
'Pne following report from the Council's Solicitor in above
matter was read •
REPORT 01" COUNCIL'S SOLICITOR,
Witih Reference to Boat Slip at Gyles' Quay.
So far as can be ascertained after inquiry tho I lifeboat
House at, Gylecs' Quay was erected/ prior to the year 1877, en-
tirely at the expense of the Royal National Lifeboat Institu-
tion, on portion of tho Coastguard Station site, which was Ad-
miralty property.
4
The Boat Slip was built at the joint expense of the Crown
and the Lifeboat Institution in 1877, for use in the launch •of
the lifeboat. The amount contril»uiecl by the Crown was idl-
chided in Che Estimates tho Beard of Works for the year
1877-1878, 'which estimates were prepauul in accordance with
the v,rovi *ions of 1 and 2 Wm. J V. , C. 33, and the amending
Sta tutes.
'l'he Pier and Harbour IVorks, viz, a Pier consisting of a
what O', a storm parapet and sloping escarpment, etc, with ex-
cava of the Harbour, were executccl by the Board of I'VorIcs
u•nder the Sea Fisaeries Act, and transferred to the, Grand Jury
in 1879. The Bom•cl point out that the boat slip in question
was not included in the tramvqfe•r. They stated Chat, if the Coun-
